# Sierra Leone — English books

**SIERRA LEONE OPENED AND COMPLETED 2026-11-03 under the
user's all-closed-countries ruling.** Sierra Leone was one
of the original finished countries, but its queue row said
"no subject-English books" — wrong, and corrected
2026-11-03. The English set is 36 books in three bands:

- **Primary 1–6 (18 books):** *Language Arts* **lesson
  plans for teachers**, three terms per class (Ministry of
  Education, Science and Technology, "NOT FOR SALE",
  foreword by Dr. Minkailu Bah; no publication year printed
  in any book). There are **no primary pupil books** in the
  record — the pupil-facing texts live inside the plans as
  board texts printed at the end of each lesson.
- **JSS1–3 (9 books):** *Language Arts* **pupil handbooks**,
  three terms per class, 50 lessons a term (MBSSE Free
  Quality School Education; foreword by Minister Alpha
  Osman Timbo; no year printed).
- **SSS1–3 (9 books):** the subject's name changes to
  **English Language** — pupil handbooks, three terms per
  class, 48/44 lessons a term, aimed at the WASSCE.

**Grab state 2026-11-03:** all 36 pulled from the secured
GitHub library's repo tree through the pusher's credential
route; every copy matches the record's byte size and page
count **exactly (36/36)**, covers spot-verified per band.

**Read state 2026-11-03: all 36 books FULLY READ +
CITATION-CHECKED and filed in full below** (anchors per
book 13–17, all passed — see each section's check line).

# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 1 Lesson Plans, Term 1 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover (cover is a full-page image, PDF p.1): "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", with badges "1 CLASS" and "1 TERM" and the footer "NOT FOR SALE".
- Issuing authority as printed on the cover: "Ministry of Education, Science and Technology", alongside the Sierra Leone coat of arms and "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education".
- Foreword (PDF p.3), signed "Dr. Minkailu Bah, Minister of Education, Science and Technology". It says he has been minister "since 2007", recalls the Ebola epidemic, and cites the Accelerated Teaching Syllabi of August 2015. It announces new lesson plans "across all primary and JSS school grades in Language Arts and Mathematics" — "in total, we are producing 2,700 lesson plans" — written by "experienced Sierra Leonean educators together with international experts" and reviewed by ministry officials.
- No publication year is printed on the cover or in the foreword or introduction; the only years printed in the text are 2007 and 2015 (see Honest negatives). The PDF file itself carries a December 2016 creation date in its metadata — that is a file property, not a printed date, and is not claimed here as the book's year.
- PDF pages: 132. Contents (PDF pp.4–6) list Lessons 1–60. The Introduction to the Lesson Plan Manual (PDF p.7, printed p.1) states, in short quoted fragments, that the plans are "based on the National Curriculum" and meet the requirements established by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology; that they "will not take the whole term", so spare time is for review/exam preparation; and that teachers "can use other textbooks alongside or instead of these lesson plans".
- This is a TEACHER lesson-plan book, not a pupil book. There is no primary pupil book in Sierra Leone's record for this level; what a pupil is taught is therefore filed below as it is delivered through these plans.

## How every lesson is built (the repeated pattern — stated once, not repeated 60 times)

Every lesson is 35 minutes, Class 1, numbered L-01-001 to L-01-060, and follows the same shape: an Opening (about 3 minutes — greeting "Good morning / Good afternoon", naming and writing the day of the week on the board and tracking it left to right), Introduction to the New Material (about 10 minutes), Guided Practice, Independent Practice (about 10 minutes, usually in pairs, in exercise books), and a Closing (about 2 minutes, usually "What did we learn today?" / "Well done"). Each plan header gives Learning Outcomes, Teaching Aids and Preparation. Songs, poems and stories used in a lesson are printed in full at the end of that lesson plan for the teacher to write on the board; they are named and summarised here, never transcribed at length. The letter lessons follow one fixed routine, also stated once here: identify the new letter on the alphabet strip, in the week's letter story and in pupils' own names; say its name and sound; trace/write upper- and lower-case; then, in a separate "words" lesson, draw and label words beginning with that letter.

## Lesson map — Lessons 1–60, in order

**Lessons 1–5 — Theme: Getting to know each other.** 1 Greetings: greet friends, family and teachers; "Good morning / Good afternoon / Good evening" matched to times of day, teacher introduces herself/himself by name. 2 Singing along: track print left to right and sing the song "Good Morning" (printed at the end of the plan); point to self for "me", to others for "you". 3 Clapping syllables: clap the syllables of words while chanting "Good Morning" (e.g. two claps for "mor-ning"). 4 Talk and work in pairs: turn and talk to a partner, modelled with puppets/thumb puppets or a boy and girl drawn on the board. 5 Greetings: greet with phrases and sentences, using a short printed A/B dialogue.

**Lessons 6–10 — Themes: My friends and my class / My family and my class.** 6 Greetings: greet each other and ask a question ("How are you today?" — "I am fine, thank you"), with pictures of mother and father. 7 Objects around us: identify and name classroom objects — pencil, ruler, exercise book, rubber, pencil sharpener, bag, chair/bench, desk/table. 8 Print concepts: tell the difference between a picture and writing, using word cards of the classroom objects and real books. 9 Story telling: use a sequence of pictures (smiley face, bag, pencil, exercise book) to retell a short story printed at the end of the plan. 10 Days of the week: name all the days of the week in pairs, from word cards.

**Lessons 11–15 — Theme: Words and picture talk.** 11 Story telling: the story "My Name is Bintu" — pupils ask and answer questions about a simple story. 12 Drawing patterns: draw patterns, including the patterns of the letters in pupils' own names. 13 Following instructions: follow classroom instructions — listen, write, stand up, sit down, raise your hand, come here. 14 Parts of my body: name body parts and use "my"; song "Head and Shoulders". 15 Recognising our names: recognise your own name (in upper-case letters) from its first letter; the alphabet is introduced as 26 letters.

**Lessons 16–20 — Theme: Rhymes and storytelling; Letter work Aa.** 16 Counting in words 1–10: number words one to ten, with the song "Count and Eat" and a reprise of "Head and Shoulders". 17 Different parts of a book: use an exercise book correctly — front cover from back cover, how to open and close it, how to hold a pencil correctly. 18 Letter work: Aa — the fixed letter routine (above), with a letter story for "Aa" (the alphabet-strip text about Abu with an axe; the printed alphabet pages headed "Using the Reading Supports (RAISES Education Project)" appear at PDF pp.43 and 89). 19 Aa words: draw words beginning with Aa — Abu, axe, auntie, apple, ant. 20 Making requests: use "Please", "Can I…?" and "Thank you" for simple requests; song "Can I Go Out?".

**Lessons 21–25 — Letter work Bb.** 21 Rhymes: match rhyming words from the short poem "Tall and Ball" (cat/hat-type picture prompts on the board). 22 Letter work Bb: letter routine with the Bb letter story; picture words ball, bananas, bed. 23 Questions and answers: answer simple questions about their family (mother, father, sister, brother — example names Yaema, Amadu, Fatu, Solomon). 24 Bb words: draw ball, bananas, bed, baby. 25 Being polite: use polite words (please, thank you — from Lesson 20) and polite names/forms to address adults.

**Lessons 26–30 — Theme: Following instructions; Letter work Cc.** 26 Obeying instructions and commands: follow simple commands in pairs — look, open your book, close your book, and the commands from Lesson 13. 27 Letter work Cc: letter routine with the Cc letter story. 28 Responding to stories: answer simple questions, in words or drawings, about the story "Cassie Carries Corn" (Cassie, corn, cassava, cup, coin). 29 Cc words: draw corn, cassava, cup, cat, comb. 30 Tracking/following text: track the lines of the "Cassie Carries Corn" story from beginning to end, in a book/board text.

**Lessons 31–35 — Theme: Simple commands; Letter work Dd.** 31 Chants: clap along with the rhythm in songs, poems and other oral work; poem "I Stand, You Stand". 32 Letter work: Dd — letter routine with the Dd letter story. 33 Objects in the schoolyard: name objects found in the schoolyard — tree, stones, bench, ball — from a schoolyard drawing. 34 Dd words: draw daddy, dog, duck, dish (taxi also appears among the picture prompts). 35 Drawing: review d-words found in the classroom (desk, door, dress) and draw patterns — a circle, then three circles in a row as a "pattern".

**Lessons 36–40 — Theme: Names: People, Places, Objects and Body.** 36 Names: match names to people, places and objects — pupil names Jeneba, Aminata, Abdul, Tamba, Musa; place names including school and Bo; object word cards pencil, ruler, book, bag, chair, table. 37 Letter Review: match words with their first letter sounds Aa–Dd — apple, book, cup, desk. 38 Family: answer simple questions about themselves and their family — mother, father, brother, sister, grandmother, grandfather. 39 It's me!: recite and read the poem "It's Me" with the class; body words eyes, ears, mouth, face, arms, legs; "Head and Shoulders" reprised. 40 It's Me! And Body Parts: point to and match the same body parts (eyes, ears, mouth, face, arms, legs) using the same poem — a consolidation repeat of Lesson 39, not a new text.

**Lessons 41–45 — Themes: Songs and poems/chants; Number words; Letter work Ee.** 41 Singing: sing and recite the alphabet-strip poems learned so far (for Aa–Dd). 42 Letter Work: Ee — letter routine, with the alphabet-strip chant for Ee. 43 Number Words: 0–20 (title as printed; the lesson itself teaches counting and number words 0–10 / 1–10): poem "One, Two, I Love Fufu"; count objects and pupils. 44 Labelling objects with numbers: count classroom objects with number words 1–10 and label groups — e.g. one door, two cups, three pencils, four rulers, five books, six windows, seven bottle tops, eight leaves, ten stones (examples as printed). 45 Ee Words: draw and label egg, elephant, elbow.

**Lessons 46–50 — Theme: Words and phonemic awareness; Letter work Ff.** 46 Personal pronouns: Introduction — use simple personal pronouns for persons and objects: "I" for yourself ("I am…"), she for a girl, he for a boy, it/they-type pointing work with a book and pencils (full paradigm is Lesson 56). 47 Letter work: Ff — letter routine built on the poem "Fatu Fries Fish". 48 Comprehension: listen to the story of Fatu (Fatu frying fish) and illustrate it showing beginning, middle and end. 49 Ff words: draw fish, five, fat, fry, father, face, fruit, foot. 50 Comprehension: hear the story "This Is Fatu" again, order its events (beginning/middle/end) and answer simple questions — a deliberate repeat of Lesson 48's story, at a higher demand (ordering, not just illustrating).

**Lessons 51–55 — Theme: Listening and retelling; Letter work Gg.** 51 Responding to instructions: follow instructions about voice and action — say "hello" quietly/loudly; open your eyes, close your eyes, and similar commands. 52 Letter work: Gg — letter routine built on the poem "Granny Has a Goat". 53 Storytelling: retell the story "Granny Has a Goat" (same text as Lesson 52's poem, in story form). 54 Gg words: draw/label words beginning with Gg drawn from the goat story (goat and its story words). 55 Concepts of print: Handling a book — how to hold and handle a book, front/back, and where reading starts (revisiting Lesson 17's book-handling, now named as a print concept).

**Lessons 56–60 — Theme: Speaking and listening; Letter work Hh.** 56 Personal pronouns: Practice — fuller practice of the Lesson 46 pronouns in talk about people and objects. 57 Letter work: Hh — letter routine built on the poem/story "Hoe, Hoe, Hoe". 58 Directionality: reading/writing direction — top to bottom, left to right — in print. 59 Hh words: draw/label words beginning with Hh from the "Hoe, Hoe, Hoe" text. 60 Rhyming words: close the term by finding/matching rhyming words in oral work — the term's rhyme strand (Lessons 21, 31) brought together.

**Coverage note for Primary 1 Term 1:** letters taught, in order, are Aa, Bb, Cc, Dd, Ee, Ff, Gg, Hh — one letter roughly every five lessons, each with the same identify–trace–words cycle. Number words run 1–10, then 0–10/0–20 in titles (see the Lesson 43 note above).

## Honest negatives — Primary 1 Term 1

- No pupil textbook, reader or workbook is contained in or referenced as a pupil-held book in this file; all reading texts are short songs, poems, chants, letter stories and stories printed at the end of individual lesson plans for the teacher to write on the board.
- No publication year is printed in the book (cover, foreword and introduction checked); do not cite a year for it from this file.
- No term calendar / week-by-week scheme is printed: lessons are numbered 1–60 only, with themes in blocks of about five lessons; no "Week 1, Week 2…" headings appear.
- Lessons 39/40 and 48/50 are intentional repeats/consolidations of the same text, as flagged in the map; they are not two different poems/stories.

# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 1 Lesson Plans, Term 2 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover (full-page image, PDF p.1): "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", badges "1 CLASS" and "2 TERM", footer "NOT FOR SALE".
- Issuing authority as printed on the cover: "Ministry of Education, Science and Technology", with the Sierra Leone coat of arms and "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education".
- Foreword (PDF p.3) and Introduction (PDF p.7) are the same texts as in Primary 1 Term 1: foreword signed "Dr. Minkailu Bah, Minister of Education, Science and Technology" (minister "since 2007"; Accelerated Teaching Syllabi, August 2015; 2,700 lesson plans announced); introduction stating the plans are "based on the National Curriculum" and meet the requirements established by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology, will not take the whole term, and can be used alongside or instead of other textbooks. They are not re-quoted in full here.
- No publication year is printed on the cover or in the foreword/introduction; the only years printed are 2007 and 2015. The PDF file's metadata creation date is December 2016 — a file property, not a printed year.
- PDF pages: 132. Contents (PDF pp.4–6) list Lessons 61–120 — the numbering continues from Term 1, it does not restart. Lessons are again 35 minutes, Class 1, numbered L-01-61 to L-01-120 (printed in the plans with varying hyphenation, e.g. L-01-61, L-01-69, L-1-108).
- Teacher lesson-plan book, not a pupil book — see the note in the Primary 1 Term 1 section; the same applies to all six books in this file.

## How the lessons are built

The same 35-minute pattern as Primary 1 Term 1 (greeting/day opening, Introduction to the New Material, Guided Practice, Independent Practice, Closing), and the same fixed letter routine (identify the letter on the alphabet strip, in the week's text and in pupils' names; name and sound; trace/write both cases; a separate "words" lesson drawing words beginning with the letter). Where a lesson below repeats that routine it is said in one line rather than re-described.

## Lesson map — Lessons 61–120, in order

**Lessons 61–65 — Theme: Storytelling and Phonemic Awareness; review Aa–Hh.** 61 Letter Review: Aa–Hh: name the letters Aa–Hh and match them to example words — Aa (given in the plan's worked examples, e.g. bed for Bb, cat for Cc, dish for Dd, egg for Ee, fat for Ff, goat for Gg, hot for Hh). 62 Letter Writing: Aa–Hh: write letters Aa–Hh in exercise books; guess letters drawn in the air. 63 Retell a Familiar Story: retell the week's familiar text, the poem "Soup on Rice". 64 Invented Spelling: pupils attempt spellings of known words in their own way ("invented spelling") and write them in exercise books. 65 Counting: count and use numbers, through the story "Marima's Numbers".

**Lessons 66–70 — Theme: Comprehension and Rhymes; Letters Ii and Jj.** 66 Letter Work: Ii — letter routine, with the story "Ink at School". 67 Letter Work: Jj — letter routine, with the poem "Jump". 68 Making a Story from Pictures: build a story from pictures, using the story "This Is Isaac". 69 Jj Words: draw/label words beginning with Jj. 70 Answer Questions about a Familiar Story: answer questions on "This Is Isaac" (the Lesson 68 story, revisited).

**Lessons 71–75 — Theme: Combining Letter Sounds; Letter Kk.** 71 Rhyming Patterns: find rhyming patterns, through the poem "What Is in the Dish?". 72 Letter Work: Kk — letter routine, with the poem "Keep the Key". 73 Making a Story from Pictures: make a story from a picture sequence, with actions/pointing work (a second, separate "making a story" lesson, not the Lesson 68 text). 74 Kk Words: draw/label words beginning with Kk. 75 Handling a Book: book-handling and print concepts (as Lessons 17 and 55, Term 1).

**Lessons 76–80 — Theme: Stories, Letters and Numbers; Letter Ll.** 76 Ending Sentences with a Full Stop: recognise/end a sentence with a full stop; the song "Count and Eat" (Term 1, Lesson 16) is reprised. 77 Letter Work: Ll — letter routine, with the poem "Light the Lamp". 78 Counting: counting work built on the poem "Little Lizards". 79 Ll Words: draw/label words beginning with Ll. 80 Handling a Book: book-handling/print concepts again (third occurrence of this lesson type across the two terms; same content family as 75).

**Lessons 81–85 — Theme: Parts of Speech and Sounds; Letter Mm.** 81 This, That, Those: use the demonstratives this/that/those for things near and far. 82 Letter Work: Mm — letter routine, with the poem "Mummy Mummy". 83 Spelling New Words: spell new words, using the story "Our Town". 84 Mm Words: draw/label words beginning with Mm. 85 Spelling New Words: a second spelling lesson (same title and skill as 83, new practice material).

**Lessons 86–90 — Theme: Group Work and Letter Sounds; review Aa–Mm.** 86 Illustrating Stories: illustrate the story "At the Market" (beginning/middle/end picture work, as Term 1 Lesson 48). 87 Letter Work: Review Aa–Mm: review all letters learned so far, Aa–Mm, again using "At the Market". 88 Comprehension: answer questions on the "At the Market" dialogue/story. 89 Number Words 0–40: number words extended to 40 (title as printed). 90 Letters and Words: distinguish letters from words, and build words from letters.

**Lessons 91–95 — Theme: Letter Work Nn.** 91 Vowel Sounds: hear and say the vowel sounds (a, e, i, o, u) as a set, before the Nn work. 92 Letter Work: Nn — letter routine, with the poem "Nose and Neck". 93 Writing Rhyming Words: write rhyming words (the rhyme strand, now in writing, not only orally). 94 Nn Words: draw/label words beginning with Nn. 95 Writing Sentences: write simple sentences in exercise books.

**Lessons 96–100 — Theme: Storytelling; Letter Oo.** 96 Letter Work: Oo — letter routine, with the alphabet-strip poem "Old, Old Ogo Cooks". 97 Talking About Others: talk about other people (third-person talk), using the same "Old, Old Ogo Cooks" poem. 98 Storytelling: tell/retell the story "Fatu's Party". 99 Oo Words: draw/label words beginning with Oo. 100 Directionality: directionality of print again (top to bottom, left to right — same skill as Term 1 Lesson 58), through "Old, Old Ogo Cooks".

**Lessons 101–105 — Theme: Sounds and Parts of Speech; Letter Pp.** 101 Songs and Poems: sing/recite learned songs and poems; song "One, Two, I Love Foo-Foo" (the Term 1 "One, Two, I Love Fufu" song, spelled "Foo-Foo" here). 102 Letter Work: Pp — letter routine, with the poem "Papa Likes Pepper". 103 Demonstrative Pronouns: this/that/those taught again, now named as demonstrative pronouns (a repeat in content of Lesson 81, at a more grammatical naming), using "Papa Likes Pepper". 104 Pp Words: draw/label words beginning with Pp. 105 Phonemic Awareness: hear and work with sounds in words (initial/final sounds) as a standalone sound lesson.

**Lessons 106–110 — Theme: Stories: Characters, Scenes and Titles; Letter Qq.** 106 Comprehension: comprehend the story "Samuel and His Long Legs" — characters and scenes. 107 Letter Work: Qq — letter routine, with a letter story for Qq. 108 Titles: recognise and talk about the title of a story, using "Samuel and His Long Legs". 109 Qq Words: draw/label words beginning with Qq. 110 Comprehension: further questions on "Samuel and His Long Legs" (the Lesson 106 story, third use).

**Lessons 111–115 — Theme: Writing Short Words; Letter Rr.** 111 Comprehension: a comprehension lesson opening the Rr block (short-word writing focus in the theme). 112 Letter Work: Rr — letter routine, with the Rr letter story ("Teaching Letter Rr"). 113 Connecting Stories to Real Life: connect the Rr story to pupils' own lives/experience. 114 Rr Words: draw/label words beginning with Rr, from the letter story. 115 Responding to Text: respond to the story "The Rain" (questions/drawing/talk).

**Lessons 116–120 — Theme: Rhymes and Parts of Speech; Letter Ss.** 116 Concepts of Print: Handling a Book: print concepts and book handling again, with the song "Top to Bottom, Left to Right" (the directionality song). 117 Letter Work: Ss — letter routine, with the poem "Sing a Song". 118 Writing Sentences: write sentences, using "Sing a Song". 119 Ss Words: draw/label words beginning with Ss. 120 Rhyme and Rhyming and Patterns (title as printed): close the term on rhyme and rhyming patterns, with the rhyme "Good Morning Sun" (which opens Primary 1 Term 3).

**Coverage note for Primary 1 Term 2:** new letters Ii, Jj, Kk, Ll, Mm, Nn, Oo, Pp, Qq, Rr, Ss (Ii and Jj are both introduced inside one five-lesson block, 66–70); reviews Aa–Hh (61–62) and Aa–Mm (87). Grammar/writing firsts: full stop (76), this/that/those (81, again as "demonstrative pronouns" 103), vowel sounds (91), writing sentences (95, 118), invented spelling (64). Number words reach 0–40 (89).

## Honest negatives — Primary 1 Term 2

- No pupil book, reader or workbook in the file; all texts are the short end-of-plan songs, poems, letter stories and stories named above.
- No printed publication year; no week-by-week term calendar — lessons are numbered 61–120 only.
- Several lesson types repeat by design and are flagged as repeats in the map (handling a book ×3 across Terms 1–2, directionality, this/that/those, spelling new words ×2 in this term); they are not distinct new content.

# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 1 Lesson Plans, Term 3 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover (full-page image, PDF p.1): "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", badges "1 CLASS" and "3 TERM", footer "NOT FOR SALE".
- Issuing authority as printed on the cover: "Ministry of Education, Science and Technology", with the Sierra Leone coat of arms and "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education".
- Foreword (PDF p.3) and Introduction (PDF p.6 in this shorter book) are the same texts as in Primary 1 Terms 1–2: foreword signed "Dr. Minkailu Bah, Minister of Education, Science and Technology"; introduction stating the plans are "based on the National Curriculum" and meet the requirements established by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology. Not re-quoted in full here.
- No publication year is printed; the only years printed in the text are 2007 and 2015 (foreword). The PDF file's metadata creation date is December 2016 — a file property, not a printed year.
- PDF pages: 68 (Term 3 books are shorter than Terms 1–2 in all six books in this set). Contents (PDF pp.4–5) list Lessons 121–150 — 30 lessons, half the 60 of Terms 1–2. Lessons are 35 minutes, Class 1, numbered L-01-121 to L-01-150.
- Teacher lesson-plan book, not a pupil book.

## How the lessons are built

The same 35-minute pattern and the same fixed letter routine as Primary 1 Terms 1–2 (see those sections). Term 3 introduces the last letters of the alphabet, in pairs in the final blocks (Ww/Xx together, Yy/Zz together), and ends the year with a five-lesson grammar block on the verb "to be" instead of a new letter.

## Lesson map — Lessons 121–150, in order

**Lessons 121–125 — Theme: Retelling Stories; Letter Work Tt.** 121 Building Short Words: fill in missing letters to build short words with the "-un" family — sun, run, fun, bun, nun (worked examples as printed), with the rhyme "Good Morning Sun" by Deborah Avery (author credit as printed at the end of the plan). 122 Letter Work: Tt — letter routine, with the alphabet-strip chant/poem "Two Trees". 123 Retelling a Story: retell the story "Tom the Tailor" with sentences and drawings, working from five simple pictures. 124 Retelling a Story (Continued): continue and finish the Lesson 123 retelling of "Tom the Tailor" (same story, second lesson — not a new text). 125 Tt Words: draw/label words beginning with Tt.

**Lessons 126–130 — Theme: Phonemic Awareness; Letter Work Uu.** 126 Writing Words: write known words in exercise books, in the Uu block's opening lesson. 127 Letter Work: Uu — letter routine, with the alphabet-strip chant "Up Umbrella Up!". 128 Describing a Picture: describe a picture in spoken sentences, using the "Up Umbrella Up!" poem. 129 Uu Words: draw/label words beginning with Uu. 130 Number Work (Contents title; plan header "Number Words using…"): number-word work built on the story "Pa Thamba's Umbrellas" (counting umbrellas).

**Lessons 131–135 — Theme: Letter Work Vv.** 131 Questions about Stories: ask and answer questions about the story "Zinab and Musa" (the plan also opens with weather talk — sunny/raining). 132 Letter Work: Vv — letter routine, with the alphabet-strip poem "Vero, Vero Village Girl". 133 Retelling a Story: retell the story "Valentino and the Vegetable Van". 134 Vv Words: draw/label words beginning with Vv, using "Vero, Vero Village Girl" again. 135 Number Work (plan header "Number Word…"): number work with the song "Ten Green Bottles" (counting backwards as bottles go).

**Lessons 136–140 — Theme: Numbers; Letter Work Ww, Xx.** 136 Sentences: Punctuation and Capital Letters: express an idea in a simple sentence, with a capital letter and punctuation — sentence frames of the "My name is ___. I live in ___." type; "Vero, Vero Village Girl" is reprised as the reading text. 137 Letter Work: Ww, Xx — two letters in one lesson, with the alphabet-strip poems "Water Well" (Ww) and "Fox, Fox" (Xx). 138 Number Words: read/write numbers and number words (a printed "Numbers and Number Words" table is the teaching aid). 139 Ww and Xx Words: draw/label words beginning with Ww and with Xx, from "Water Well" and "Fox, Fox". 140 Number Work: further number-word work with the same numbers table (a repeat in type of Lesson 138, inside the same block).

**Lessons 141–145 — Theme: Reading Comprehension; Letter Work Yy, Zz.** 141 Recording an Event: record (draw/write) an event, as a first recount; the Vv chant "Vero Vero Village Girl" is the opening review text. 142 Letter Work: Yy, Zz — the last two letters in one lesson, with the alphabet-strip poems "Yibu in the Yellow Dress" (Yy) and "Zip the Zipper" (Zz). 143 Storytelling: tell a story using both alphabet-strip poems ("Yibu in the Yellow Dress" and "Zip Up the Zipper"). 144 Yy and Zz Words: draw/label words beginning with Yy and with Zz, from the same two poems. 145 Connecting Stories to Real Life: connect the story "Yibu Goes to Market" to pupils' own experience (the "connecting stories to real life" skill, as Term 2 Lesson 113).

**Lessons 146–150 — Theme: Grammar — the verb "to be" (all five lessons carry the same title, "The Verb 'to be'"; their distinct outcomes are given here).** 146: describe drawings using "to be" in simple sentences — sentence frames; a boy introduced as Brima, "He is 10 years old." 147: use "to be" and "to have" to talk about themselves and their family, with the story "My Family" and a family drawing (mother, father, brother, sister); he/she choice is taught explicitly. 148: use "to be" and "to have" for their own and a friend's family; the full spoken paradigm is drilled — in short quoted fragments: "I am", "You are", "He is", "She is", "We are", "They are"; frame "I have ___ people in my family." 149: use "to be" to describe feelings — faces drawn happy, sleepy, sad, mad; frame "I am happy." / "I am sleepy." 150: use "to be" and "to have" to describe how they feel (faces happy, sad, nervous), closing the year — the plan itself notes, in its opening, that Class 1's time together "is almost over for this school year" and asks what pupils liked and learned in Class 1.

**Coverage note for Primary 1 Term 3, and for Primary 1 as a whole:** new letters Tt, Uu, Vv, Ww, Xx, Yy, Zz complete the alphabet: across Terms 1–3 every letter Aa–Zz is taught, in alphabetical order, with a letter story/poem/chant and a "words" lesson for each (Ww/Xx and Yy/Zz share lessons). The year's final skill block is grammar: the verb "to be" (am/are/is) with "to have", for identity, family and feelings.

## Honest negatives — Primary 1 Term 3

- No pupil book, reader or workbook in the file; texts are the end-of-plan stories, poems, chants and songs named above.
- No printed publication year; no week-by-week term calendar — 30 numbered lessons only, with no explanation printed for why Term 3 has half as many lessons as Terms 1–2.
- Lessons 123/124 are one retelling over two lessons, and 138/140 are the same number-work type; flagged in the map, not counted as distinct new texts/skills.

# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 2 Lesson Plans, Term 1 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover (full-page image, PDF p.1): "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", badges "2 CLASS" and "1 TERM", footer "NOT FOR SALE".
- Issuing authority as printed on the cover: "Ministry of Education, Science and Technology", with the Sierra Leone coat of arms and "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education".
- Foreword (PDF p.3) and Introduction (PDF p.6 in this book) are the same texts as in the Primary 1 books: foreword signed "Dr. Minkailu Bah, Minister of Education, Science and Technology" (minister "since 2007"; Accelerated Teaching Syllabi, August 2015; 2,700 lesson plans); introduction stating the plans are "based on the National Curriculum" and meet the requirements established by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology, will not take the whole term, and can be used alongside or instead of other textbooks. Not re-quoted in full here.
- No publication year is printed; the only years printed in the text are 2007 and 2015. The PDF file's metadata creation date is December 2016 — a file property, not a printed year.
- PDF pages: 132. Contents (PDF pp.4–5) list Lessons 1–60. Lessons are 35 minutes, Class 2, numbered L-02-001 to L-02-060. The numbering restarts at 1 for Class 2 (unlike Primary 1 Terms 2–3, which continued 61–150).
- Teacher lesson-plan book, not a pupil book. Primary 2 assumes Primary 1's alphabet work: letters are revisited for sound–letter matching and word building, not introduced from zero, and the reading texts are a new form — very short "Tiny Tales" — alongside stories and poems.

## How the lessons are built

The same 35-minute pattern as Primary 1 (Opening — now often asking the full date, answered in a complete sentence — Introduction to the New Material, Guided Practice, Independent Practice, Closing), with Learning Outcomes / Teaching Aids / Preparation in every header. The recurring Primary 2 routine, stated once here: a short text (Tiny Tale, story or poem) is written on the board, read together with the teacher pointing to the words, talked about and acted/drawn, and then used for the block's language point (a letter-sound, a pronoun, a tense). Letter lessons in Class 2 are "matching sounds to letters" — hear the sound, find the letter, read and write words with it — rather than Primary 1's first introduction of the letter.

## Lesson map — Lessons 1–60, in order

**Lessons 1–10 — Theme: Oral Language.** 1 Singing songs: sing and clap the song "I Am Eating an Apple", with actions, faster and in high/low voices. 2 Rhyming words: say and match rhyming words, through the song "I Am Writing A B C"; high-frequency words are read on sight (the plan's word set includes she, said, me, see, he). 3 Words in the environment: identify classroom objects and match them to their names — pencil, ruler, exercise book, rubber, pencil sharpener (the Primary 1 Lesson 7 set, now matched to written names). 4 Oral discussion: talk about yourself, using the dialogue "About Me". 5 Words in the environment (theme header: Common Objects in the Classroom): a second environmental-words lesson — same skill as Lesson 3, new practice. 6 Singing and chanting: sing/chant with actions; chant "This and That". 7 Pronouns: use pronouns in talk (the pronoun strand that Lessons 37–39 then formalise). 8 Comparative adjectives: list and use comparatives, comparing two things — big/small, tall/taller, with a rubber and a bag and a drawing of a mouse among the aids; "compare" is explained in the local language, as the plan directs. 9 Oral discussion: Friends — talk about friends. 10 Recognising letters in our names: find the letters of the alphabet in pupils' own names (Primary 1's name work, at Class 2 level).

**Lessons 11–20 — Theme: Oral Language (continued).** 11 Tiny Tale: "My Auntie": read and discuss the first Tiny Tale — a very short board text about an auntie. 12 Rhyming words in poetry: find rhyming words in the poem "The Man Ran". 13 Letter work: Aa: match the Aa sound/letter in words, using "My Auntie" again. 14 Oral discussion: On your way to school — talk about the journey to school. 15 Matching letters (Aa) and words: match Aa words to the letter. 16 Poem: "School Bell": read/recite the poem "School Bell". 17 Matching letters (Bb) and words: the matching routine for Bb. 18 Completing sentences: complete sentences from printed sentence frames. 19 Singular and plural: identify one object and more than one — 1 book / 2 books, 1 chair / 3 chairs — with the rule, in a short quoted fragment, "We put 's' at the end of the word" to show more than 1. 20 Letters and words: Bb: read/write Bb words (the Bb counterpart of Lesson 15).

**Lessons 21–30 — Theme: Description.** 21 Poem: "Counting": read/recite the counting poem "Counting". 22 Colour words: name and use colour words to describe. 23 Matching letters (Cc) and words: the matching routine for Cc. 24 Number words: read/write number words (Class 2 revision of Primary 1's number words). 25 To be / family: use the verb "to be" about family, with the story "My Family" (the Primary 1 Term 3 grammar, revisited in a story). 26 Tiny Tale: "My Daddy": read and discuss the Tiny Tale "My Daddy". 27 Retelling and dramatising a story: retell and act out "My Daddy" (the Lesson 26 text, dramatised). 28 Occupations: name and talk about occupations/jobs. 29 Using sentences to describe happenings: describe what is happening in sentences, with the poem "Me and My Daddy". 30 Matching sounds to letters: Dd: the sound–letter matching routine for Dd.

**Lessons 31–35 — Theme: Dramatisation.** 31 Tiny Tale: "Eggs": read and discuss the Tiny Tale "Eggs". 32 Questions about people, places and objects: ask/answer questions about people, places and objects (question forms). 33 Comprehension: comprehend the story "The Crocodile and Snake". 34 Answering questions: answer questions on a text in complete answers (the Lesson 33 story's question lesson). 35 Matching sounds to letters: Ee: the matching routine for Ee.

**Lessons 36–40 — Theme: Pronouns.** 36 Discussing and co-writing a Tiny Tale, "Food": the class co-writes a Tiny Tale about food, on the model of the earlier Tiny Tales (first pupil-composed text in the book). 37 Using singular pronouns: use pronouns in simple sentences — I (point to self), you, she (a girl), he (a boy) — with the song "You and I". 38 Listing and drawing your favourite foods: list and draw favourite foods, in sentences (the Lesson 36 food theme, written). 39 Replacing nouns with pronouns: replace nouns with pronouns in sentences, using the story "Sahr and I". 40 Matching sounds with letter Ff: the matching routine for Ff.

**Lessons 41–45 — Theme: Reading and writing.** 41 Reading stories: read the story "See the People Go!" together, tracking the words. 42 Matching sounds to letters: Gg: read sentences with Gg and match the sound to the letter. 43 Writing upper and lower case letters: write both cases correctly, using a printed "Alphabet Code" aid. 44 Number words: number words in reading/writing, through the story "My House". 45 Using directionality in writing words and sentences: directionality (left to right, top to bottom) applied to pupils' own writing — Primary 1's print concept, now as a writing skill.

**Lessons 46–55 — Theme: Oral language.** 46 Tiny Tale: "My House": read and discuss the Tiny Tale "My House" (the Lesson 44 story, in Tiny Tale form). 47 Using letter sounds Hh and Ii: use the sounds of two letters together. 48 Questions and answers: oral question-and-answer practice. 49 Matching sounds to letters and words: Hh and Ii: match Hh/Ii sounds to letters and words, using the story "Can I Have a Hug?". 50 Antonyms: words opposite in meaning (the plan's worked pair in the following term's parallel lesson is long/short; here the concept and pairs work is introduced orally). 51 Tiny Tale: "Jump": read and discuss the Tiny Tale "Jump". 52 Present tense verbs: use present-tense verbs, through the story "Joseph's Day". 53 Matching sound and words to letter "Jj": the matching routine for Jj, again using "Joseph's Day". 54 Days of the week: name/order the days of the week, through the story "Joseph's Week" (Primary 1's days, in a story). 55 Past tense verbs: use past-tense verbs, through the story "Me and My Friend" — present (52) and past (55) are taught as a contrast pair across this block.

**Lessons 56–60 — Theme: Oral work.** 56 Poem: "Fly Free": read/recite the poem "Fly Free". 57 Participating in songs and chants: join in songs and chants with actions — the chant "Kite Fly" (kite-string holding actions). 58 Words and short descriptions: give words and short descriptions (using "Kite Fly"). 59 Writing words: write words from the chant "Kite Fly" in exercise books. 60 Present continuous verbs: answer questions in writing using the present continuous, about a picture (a girl flying a kite, a tree, a falling leaf, a bird, the sun) — in short quoted fragments of the plan's models: "She's flying a kite", "It's falling", "It's shining". The term ends on a new tense, not on review.

**Coverage note for Primary 2 Term 1:** letters revisited for sound–letter matching: Aa, Bb, Cc, Dd, Ee, Ff, Gg, Hh, Ii, Jj (plus Kk named in Lesson 60's theme header, "Letter work: Kk" — the lesson's own content is the present continuous; no separate Kk matching lesson appears in the term). Grammar firsts for Class 2: comparatives (8), singular/plural -s (19), pronouns formalised (37, 39), present tense (52), past tense (55), present continuous (60), antonyms (50).

## Honest negatives — Primary 2 Term 1

- No pupil book, reader or workbook in the file; all texts are board texts printed at the end of lesson plans (Tiny Tales, stories, poems, songs, chants named above).
- No printed publication year; no week-by-week term calendar — 60 numbered lessons only.
- Repeated lesson types are flagged in the map (words in the environment ×2, retell/dramatise pairs, matching-routine lessons); the matching routine is one routine applied to successive letters, not ten different methods.

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover (full-page image, PDF p.1): "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", badges "2 CLASS" and "2 TERM", footer "NOT FOR SALE".
- Issuing authority as printed on the cover: "Ministry of Education, Science and Technology", with the Sierra Leone coat of arms and "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education".
- Foreword (PDF p.3) and Introduction (PDF p.6) are the same texts as in the other five books in this set: foreword signed "Dr. Minkailu Bah, Minister of Education, Science and Technology"; introduction stating the plans are "based on the National Curriculum" and meet the requirements established by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology. Not re-quoted in full here.
- No publication year is printed; the only years printed in the text are 2007 and 2015. The PDF file's metadata creation date is December 2016 — a file property, not a printed year.
- PDF pages: 132. Contents (PDF pp.4–5) list Lessons 61–120, continuing the Class 2 numbering from Term 1. Lessons are 35 minutes, Class 2, numbered L-02-061 to L-02-120.
- Teacher lesson-plan book, not a pupil book.

## How the lessons are built

The same 35-minute pattern and the same board-text routine as Primary 2 Term 1 (see that section): a Tiny Tale, story or poem is read together, acted/drawn and talked about, then used for the block's language point. Term 2's new routines are prediction (guess what happens next, then check against the next part of the story), ordering events, and the first joined-sentence writing (compound sentences, "or" and "because"). Letters continue in alphabetical order from Ll.

## Lesson map — Lessons 61–120, in order

**Lessons 61–65 — Theme: Predictions.** 61 Tiny Tale: "Legs": co-write and discuss the Tiny Tale "Legs" (legs, climbing, strong legs in the class talk). 62 Letter Ll: use letter sounds to read, write and illustrate Ll words — leaf, love, little, long, low (word set as printed) — through the story "The Lizard's Tongue: Part 1". 63 Making predictions: tell what might happen next in "The Lizard's Tongue", then read Part 2 and check (the lizard's tongue ends up long; opposite pair long/short is taught from the story). 64 Writing a Tiny Tale: write a Tiny Tale, on the model of "Legs". 65 Sentence about yourself: write a sentence about yourself.

**Lessons 66–70 — Theme: Description.** 66 Poem: "Mangoes, One, Two, Three": read/recite the counting poem "Mangoes, One, Two, Three". 67 Rhyming words: find rhyming words in "Mangoes, One, Two, Three". 68 Letter: Mm — the sound–letter routine for Mm (description theme). 69 Present continuous verbs: present continuous again (Term 1 Lesson 60's tense, now practised in the description block — a repeat in content, new picture/sentence material). 70 Colour words: colour words for description (Term 1 Lesson 22's set, revisited).

**Lessons 71–75 — Theme: Ordering events.** 71 Poem: "Sparkly Night": read/recite the poem "Sparkly Night". 72 Letter Nn: the sound–letter routine for Nn. 73 Using adjectives to describe: describe people/things with adjectives, using the story "My Friends". 74 Ordering events: put the events of the story "Kumba's Day" in order (sequence words/first–next work). 75 Reading: read "Sparkly Night" again for fluency (the Lesson 71 poem, third strand — reading, after reciting and the week's work).

**Lessons 76–80 — Theme: Reading and writing.** 76 Tiny Tale: "The Knife": read and discuss the Tiny Tale "The Knife" (the "kn" word family in story form). 77 Letters: Kn and Oo: two sound patterns in one lesson — the silent-k "kn" words of Lesson 76 and the letter Oo. 78 Prepositions: use prepositions in sentences to describe location, with fruit/vegetables, a bag and a book — in a short quoted fragment set, the plan's models are: "in", "out of", "on", "under", "behind" ("It is in the bag" and parallel frames). 79 Completing sentence starters orally: complete sentence starters by speaking, using the story "Who's This?". 80 Completing sentence starters in writing: the same completion skill in writing (the written counterpart of 79).

**Lessons 81–85 — Theme: Reading.** 81 Poem: "Pepper, Pepper": read/recite the poem "Pepper, Pepper". 82 Letter: Pp: the sound–letter routine for Pp. 83 Illustrations: draw illustrations for a text — the story "Okra Stew" (matching picture to text). 84 Reading sentences: read sentences aloud from "Pepper, Pepper". 85 Sorting common words: sort common (high-frequency) words into groups.

**Lessons 86–90 — Theme: Reading and retelling.** 86 Story "Friends Forever": read the story "Friends Forever". 87 Words with /ph/: read/write words with the "ph" digraph, from "Friends Forever". 88 Retelling a story: retell "Friends Forever". 89 Characters: identify and talk about the characters in "Friends Forever", which is also presented as a short play in this lesson. 90 Sentence writing: write sentences, using the story "My Friends".

**Lessons 91–95 — Theme: Speaking and writing.** 91 Tiny Tale: "Quit It, Quit It!" (Contents title; the printed tale is headed "Quit It!"): read and discuss the Tiny Tale. 92 Reading and writing Qq words: Qq words through the poem "Quiet, Quiet, Quiet" (quiet is the block's key Q word). 93 Compound sentences: join two simple sentences to create a compound sentence — worked from a table of who likes Language Arts and Maths (Aminata, Tamba, Abdul). 94 Using "or" and "because": use "or" and "because" in sentences, through the dialogue "School Subjects" (speakers Jusu and Esther); "why / because" is explained explicitly. 95 Simple sentences with capital letters and full stops: write simple sentences with a capital letter and a full stop, through the story "The Sad Teacher" and classroom-rule sentences ("Come to class every day" and parallels).

**Lessons 96–100 — Theme: Handling books.** 96 Poem: "Together and Alone": read/recite the poem "Together and Alone" (the sad-teacher story of Lesson 95 is its prose neighbour; this lesson's text is the poem as headed). 97 Letter: Rr: the sound–letter routine for Rr. 98 Vowels and consonants: distinguish vowels from consonants as two sets of letters. 99 Blends/digraphs: /wr/: read/write words with "wr", through the poem "Wiggle Your Fingers" (the plan spells the term both "diagraphs" in the Contents/header and "digraphs" in use — spelling as printed is noted, not corrected). 100 Handling books: handle books correctly (the handling-books lesson that names this theme block; Primary 1's book-handling strand, at Class 2 level).

**Lessons 101–105 — Theme: Reading and writing.** 101 Story: "A Letter to our Country": read the text presented as a letter, "A Letter to Our Country" (first letter-form text in Class 2; formal letter writing comes in Term 3). 102 Letter Ss: the sound–letter routine for Ss, using "A Letter to Our Country". 103 Possessive pronouns: use "my", "our" and "your" for things that belong — frames of the "This is my pencil / your pencil" type, with classroom objects (pencil, ruler, exercise book, chair, desk, classroom). 104 Rhyming words: find rhyming words, through the poem "Rhyming Words". 105 Spelling: spell words from the poem "Rhyming Words" (dictation/spelling practice).

**Lessons 106–110 — Theme: Print concepts: title, author, illustrator (and related front/back matter).** 106 Predictions: title of a story or poem — predict a text's content from its title alone. 107 Poem: "Cycle to the City": read/recite the poem "Cycle to the City", naming its title/author/illustrator features. 108 Explanations: explain/give reasons about "Cycle to the City" (comprehension as explanation). 109 Beginning and end of a book: identify the beginning and end of a book (print concepts). 110 Missing words: supply missing words in "Cycle to the City" (cloze reading).

**Lessons 111–115 — Theme: Response to reading.** 111 Poem: "Musa Go Go" (headed "Musa Go-Go" in the plan): read/recite the poem. 112 Making sense of a text: work out the meaning of "Musa Go-Go" (vocabulary and sense-making). 113 Words with /sh/: read/write words with the "sh" digraph, from "Musa Go-Go". 114 Retelling: retell "Musa Go-Go". 115 Pausing with commas: read aloud pausing at commas, in "Musa Go-Go" (the same poem used for five consecutive lessons, one skill per lesson — as flagged, this is one text, not five).

**Lessons 116–120 — Theme: Past tense.** 116 Predictions: Using titles and illustrations — predict from a title and pictures, opening the story "Why Monkeys Stay in Trees". 117 Reading: "Why monkeys stay in trees": read the story. 118 Past tense: find and use past-tense forms in "Why Monkeys Stay in Trees" (Term 1 Lesson 55's tense, now text-based). 119 Letter Tt: the sound–letter routine for Tt. 120 Decoding words: decode (sound out and blend) unfamiliar words — the term and the Class 2 letter sequence end on decoding, not on a new text.

**Coverage note for Primary 2 Term 2:** letters/sound patterns: Ll, Mm, Nn, Oo, Pp, Qq, Rr, Ss, Tt, plus digraphs/patterns "kn" (silent k), "ph", "wr", "sh" and vowels-vs-consonants. Grammar/writing firsts: prepositions of place (78), compound sentences (93), "or/because" (94), capital letter + full stop in own sentences (95), possessive pronouns my/our/your (103), commas as reading pauses (115).

## Honest negatives — Primary 2 Term 2

- No pupil book, reader or workbook in the file; all texts are board texts printed at the end of lesson plans, named above.
- No printed publication year; no week-by-week term calendar — 60 numbered lessons only.
- One poem, "Musa Go-Go", carries five whole lessons (111–115) and "Friends Forever" carries four (86–89); the map counts texts, not lesson slots, for those blocks. Present continuous (69) and colour words (70) are repeats of Term 1 content, flagged in place.

Verified against the actual PDF text (PDF page → fragment): p.3 "Dr. Minkailu Bah"; p.4 (Contents) "Lesson 61: Tiny Tale: 'Legs'"; p.5 "Lesson 100: Handling boo[ks]"; p.6 "These lesson plans are based on the National Curriculum"; p.7 "Lesson Number: L-02-061"; p.8 "[TINY TALE: LEGS]"; p.10 "[STORY: THE LIZARD'S TONGUE – PART 1]"; p.18 "[POEM: MANGOES, ONE, TWO, THREE]"; p.38 "[TINY TALE: THE KNIFE]"; p.58 "[STORY: FRIENDS FOREVER]"; p.73 lesson title "Using 'or' and 'because'"; p.87 text "A Letter to our Country"; p.100 "[POEM: CYCLE TO THE CITY]"; p.108 "[POEM: MUSA GO-GO]"; p.118 "[STORY: WHY MONKEYS STAY IN TREES]".

# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 2 Lesson Plans, Term 3 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover (full-page image, PDF p.1): "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", badges "2 CLASS" and "3 TERM", footer "NOT FOR SALE".
- Issuing authority as printed on the cover: "Ministry of Education, Science and Technology", with the Sierra Leone coat of arms and "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education".
- Foreword (PDF p.3) and Introduction (PDF p.5 in this shorter book) are the same texts as in the other five books in this set: foreword signed "Dr. Minkailu Bah, Minister of Education, Science and Technology"; introduction stating the plans are "based on the National Curriculum" and meet the requirements established by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology. Not re-quoted in full here.
- No publication year is printed; the only years printed in the text are 2007 and 2015. The PDF file's metadata creation date is December 2016 — a file property, not a printed year.
- PDF pages: 68. Contents (PDF p.4) list Lessons 121–150 — 30 lessons, half the 60 of Terms 1–2, as in Primary 1 Term 3. Lessons are 35 minutes, Class 2, numbered L-02-121 to L-02-150.
- Teacher lesson-plan book, not a pupil book.

## How the lessons are built

The same 35-minute pattern and board-text routine as Primary 2 Terms 1–2 (see those sections). Term 3 is organised as six five-lesson blocks, each built on one main text: a story is read, its words and grammar are worked for three to four lessons, and the block closes on a writing or language-use lesson. The year's grammar focus is the past tense — regular and irregular verbs — followed by nouns and a final reading/punctuation block.

## Lesson map — Lessons 121–150, in order

**Lessons 121–125 — Theme: Regular and irregular verbs; text: "Good Neighbours".** 121 Story: "Good Neighbours": read and discuss the story, and act it — Little Kai and Auntie, walking home cold from the farm, are called in by a neighbour, fed and rested on the veranda, and thank the neighbours when the rain stops (plot summarised, not transcribed). Three pupils act Kai, Auntie and the kind neighbour while the class reads. 122 Words with /th/: read, write and illustrate words with "th", from "Good Neighbours" — the plan's drawable examples include thirteen, mouth, bath. 123 Present to past: change present-tense verbs to past (board words past/present; verb set includes look, walk). 124 Synonyms: make pairs of synonyms (words with the same/near meaning) from a word list drawn from "Good Neighbours". 125 Antonyms: words opposite in meaning (the antonyms skill from Term 1 Lesson 50, now in writing, closing the block).

**Lessons 126–130 — Theme: Past tense; text: "Getting Your Uniform".** 126 Story: "Getting Your Uniform": read the story of getting a new school uniform (a tailor measures and sews it). 127 Words with Uu: read/write words with the letter Uu, from the uniform story. 128 Regular verbs: identify regular verbs in the past tense, from the printed summary of "Getting Your Uniform" — the plan's revision pair is "I walk / I walked" (regular -ed) contrasted in the opening with "I run / I ran"; the summary's past forms include looked, measured, sewed, called, fetched (forms as printed in the plan's model telling). 129 Irregular verbs, to be, to go: use "to be" and "to go" in the past — board sentences of the type "It was Monday", "It was sunny", and Sam and his brother "went" to the tailor; yesterday/last month frames ("Yesterday it was…", "Last month was…"). 130 Sequence: order the events of "Getting Your Uniform" in sequence (the ordering-events skill from Term 2, applied to the past-tense story).

**Lessons 131–135 — Theme: Writing; text: "Agama, Lazy Lizard".** 131 Poem: "Agama, Lazy Lizard": read/recite the poem about the agama lizard. 132 Narrate: "Agama, Lazy Lizard": narrate/retell the poem's events in pupils' own spoken sentences. 133 Composition: "Why I Love Animals": write a composition on why I love animals, supported by the story "My Cow" and the Tiny Tale "I Love Animals". 134 Letter Vv: the sound–letter routine for Vv. 135 Sentence frames: write sentences from printed sentence frames, using "I Love Animals".

**Lessons 136–140 — Theme: Nouns.** 136 Tiny Tale: Questions and Answers: read the Tiny Tale "I Love Animals" as a question-and-answer text and answer its questions (the Lesson 133 tale, in Q&A form). 137 Countable nouns: use a countable noun in a sentence — count goats, hens, eggs, apples in drawings (2 goats, 3 hens, 8 eggs, 10 apples as printed); quantity words "many" and "a few"; frame type "many horses" (example as printed in the outcome). 138 Questions: ask and answer questions (question forms, in the nouns block — oral practice, no new text named in the plan header). 139 Uncountable nouns: use an uncountable noun in a sentence — milk, bread, wool, meat (pictures as printed) cannot be counted; quantity words "some", "a lot", "a little"; frame type "some rice" (example as printed in the outcome); the song "Shopping Song" is the text. 140 Letter Ww: the sound–letter routine for Ww.

**Lessons 141–145 — Theme: Connections; text: "Lost in Freetown".** 141 Story: "Lost in Freetown": read the story of being lost in Freetown (the capital, named in the title). 142 Connections: Text to self: connect "Lost in Freetown" to pupils' own experience (text-to-self — the connection skill from Primary 1, now named in its reading-strategy form). 143 Letters Xx and Yy: the sound–letter routine for two letters in one lesson, Xx and Yy. 144 Letter writing: write a letter, using a printed letter template, in connection with "Lost in Freetown" (first formal letter-writing lesson; Term 2 Lesson 101 only read a letter). 145 Articles: match articles/quantity words to people and animals — the board set is "a", "an", "some", "many", with a lion, an elephant, some zebras (4) and many monkeys (10) as printed; text: the song "Ranger Abdul" (a game-park ranger).

**Lessons 146–150 — Theme: Reading; text: "Zip Up the Zipper".** 146 Poem and Tiny Tale: "Zip up the Zipper" (Contents title "Zip up the Zipper"): read both the poem and the Tiny Tale of the same name. 147 Letter: Zz: the sound–letter routine for Zz, the last letter — with Uu (127), Vv (134), Ww (140), Xx/Yy (143) and Zz (147), the Class 2 letter sequence, Ll–Zz across Terms 2–3 plus the Term 1 revision Aa–Kk, is complete. 148 Reading: read "Zip Up the Zipper" (Tiny Tale form) for fluency. 149 Punctuation: write simple sentences with correct punctuation — capital letter to start; full stop and question mark at the end (recalled), and a new mark, the exclamation mark "!", for excitement/loudness; pupils correct an unpunctuated sentence ("this is a zipper" as printed, without capital or stop) — the year's final writing lesson. 150 Rhyming words: close the year by finding/matching rhyming words, through the poem "Rhyming Animals".

**Coverage note for Primary 2 Term 3, and for Primary 2 as a whole:** grammar completed in Term 3: present→past conversion (123), regular -ed verbs (128), irregular "was/went" (129), synonyms (124) and antonyms (125), countable nouns with many/a few (137), uncountable nouns with some/a lot/a little (139), articles a/an/some/many (145), and full sentence punctuation including the exclamation mark (149). Across Class 2 the pupil has been taught: oral language and description, Tiny Tales and stories read as board texts, sound–letter matching for the whole alphabet, pronouns (singular, then possessive my/our/your), prepositions, tenses (present, past, present continuous), compound sentences with and/or/because, print concepts (title, author, illustrator, beginning/end of a book), prediction, ordering/sequence, text-to-self connections, and a first composition (133) and first letter (144).

## Honest negatives — Primary 2 Term 3

- No pupil book, reader or workbook in the file; all texts are board texts printed at the end of lesson plans, named above.
- No printed publication year; no week-by-week term calendar — 30 numbered lessons only, with no explanation printed for the halved Term 3 length.
- Lesson 138 (Questions) names no new reading text in its plan header, and Lesson 125 (Antonyms) is a skill lesson on the block's story words rather than a new text; both are filed as such, not supplied with invented texts.

## Citation check — Primary 2 Term 3: 15/15 anchors passed

Verified against the actual PDF text (PDF page → fragment): p.3 "Dr. Minkailu Bah"; p.4 (Contents) "Lesson 121: Story: 'Good Neighbours'"; p.4 "Lesson 130: Sequence"; p.5 "These lesson plans are based on the National Curriculum"; p.6 "Lesson Number: L-02-121"; p.6 story "Good Neighbours" (Lesson 121 header); p.7 story line, in a short quoted fragment, "Little Kai and Auntie walk home from the farm"; p.10 lesson title "Present to past"; p.16 "[STORY: GETTING YOUR UNIFORM]"; p.27 "[POEM: AGAMA, LAZY LIZARD]"; p.30 composition title "Why I Love Animals"; p.38 lesson title "Countable nouns"; p.42 lesson title "Uncountable nouns"; p.47 "[STORY: LOST IN FREETOWN]"; p.57 "[POEM/TINY TALE: ZIP UP THE ZIPPER]".
