# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 3 Lesson Plans, Term 1 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover: "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", with badges "3 CLASS" and "1 TERM".
- Issuing authority as printed: Ministry of Education, Science and Technology (top right of cover), under "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education", with the Sierra Leone coat of arms. The cover is marked "NOT FOR SALE".
- Foreword (PDF p.3), signed by Dr. Minkailu Bah, Minister of Education, Science and Technology: the plans were produced after the Ebola epidemic, building on the Accelerated Teaching Syllabi of August 2015; the ministry states it produced 2,700 lesson plans across primary and JSS Language Arts and Mathematics, written by Sierra Leonean educators with international experts and reviewed by ministry officials. No publication year is printed on the cover or in the foreword (the PDF file metadata is dated 29 December 2016; that is file metadata, not a printed year).
- PDF pages: 132. Printed pagination runs to 127; lessons start on even printed pages.
- This is a TEACHER book: Sierra Leone's primary Language Arts record is lesson plans, and there are no primary pupil books in the record. Everything a pupil meets — stories, poems, rhymes, songs — is printed at the end of each lesson plan for the teacher to write on the board or read aloud.
- Structure: a Table of Contents (PDF pp.4–5), then an "Introduction to the Lesson Plan Manual" page (printed p.1) — identical in all six books read for this file — telling teachers the plans are based on the National Curriculum, that the plans will not fill the whole term (spare time is for review and exam preparation), that other textbooks may be used alongside, and giving ten working instructions (read ahead, prepare aids, review the previous lesson, follow the time allocations, mix whole-class and pair work, congratulate pupils, and so on).
- Every lesson is 35 minutes in the same shape: Opening (2 minutes), Introduction to the New Material (about 10 minutes), Guided Practice, Independent Practice, Closing (2 minutes). Each plan states a Lesson Title, Theme, Lesson Number (L-03-001 etc.), Learning Outcomes, Teaching Aids and Preparation. Lessons are numbered continuously across the school year: Term 1 = Lessons 1–60, and the plans repeatedly call the text of a five-lesson theme block "this week's" story or poem, i.e. one 35-minute lesson per school day.

## The term's lessons, in order

**Lessons 1–5 — opening oral work and first story (theme: Oral Expression, then Reading and Sentences).**
- L1 Responding to Text: Drawing (L-03-001, PDF p.7) and L2 Responding to Text: Talking with a Partner (L-03-002): pupils hear the story "Joy and the Naughty Monkeys" (credited at the end of the plan to Nathaniel Pearce, an abridged version — a girl finds a mango tree full of ripe fruit behind the house), first draw a picture in response to it, then discuss it with a partner using questions and comments.
- L3 Sounds and Letters Review: First Letter of Your Name (L-03-003): matching initial sounds and letters to the pupils' own names.
- "Describe Your Best Friend" (headed L-03-005, PDF p.13; TOC Lesson 4): describing a best friend with adjectives (tall, short, honest, caring…) and saying what they like about them.
- "Asking Questions About a Story" (headed L-03-006, PDF p.15; TOC Lesson 5): first meeting with the fable "The Dog and the Shadow" (vocabulary prepared on the board: bridge, stream, reflection, greedy); pupils learn to ask simple questions about a story. This fable returns all term (L36, L37, L44 below).

**An unlisted lesson and the numbering fault (honest negative).** At PDF pp.17–18 sits a lesson the Table of Contents does not list under its own title: "Describing Self with Adjectives" (headed L-03-004) — pupils use adjectives to describe themselves to a partner, with sentence frames about complexion, eyes and height. Because of it, the header numbers in the first ten lessons do not match the TOC: TOC Lessons 4 and 5 carry headers L-03-005 and L-03-006; "Following Directions" (TOC Lesson 6, PDF p.19) is headed "L-0 3-007" (sic); "Describing My Family" (TOC Lesson 7) is headed L-03-008; and the header L-03-009 is printed on two consecutive lessons (PDF pp.23 and 25). From L11 on, header and TOC numbers agree again. Two more header typos occur later: L13 is headed "L3-03-013". The content sequence itself is complete and in order; only the printed numbers are faulty.
- Following Directions (PDF p.19): following simple directions in the song "Crossing the Street" (a look-right, look-left road-safety song printed at the end of the plan).
- Describing My Family (PDF p.21): asking questions about a friend's family and describing one's own family, built around the rhyme "The Cat Family".
- L9–L10 Letter Sounds at the Beginning of Words (PDF pp.23, 25): matching letters and their sounds at the start of words — the same lesson title, theme and outcome printed twice in a row, the second lesson continuing the first.
- L10 in TOC numbering, Using Complete Sentences (headed L-03-010, PDF p.27): using short complete sentences to talk about oneself.

**Lessons 11–15 — first poem and first word family (theme: Reading / Reading and Shapes).**
- L11 Poem: Agama Lizard (PDF p.29) and L12 Reading Text Aloud (PDF p.31): reading an information poem about the agama lizard together, then aloud; L12's plan also prints the rhyme "Lazy Lizard", which becomes the teaching aid for the next two lessons.
- L13 Word Family: 'un' (PDF p.33) and L14 Word Family: 'un' (Continued) (PDF p.35): the year-long word-family pattern is established here — create, read and write a rime family (sun, fun, bun, run) in lesson one; in lesson two, illustrate a word from the family and label it. This two-lesson pattern repeats for every word family in Primary 3.
- L15 Shapes: Identify and Describe (PDF p.37): identifying and describing different shapes.

**Lessons 16–20 — Tiny Tales and prepositions (theme: Reading and Preposition(s)).**
- L16 Tiny Tale: Forest Animals (PDF p.39): the class co-writes and discusses a very short "Tiny Tale" from a printed frame, about forest animals and where they are.
- L17–L18 Word Family: 'ee' (PDF pp.41, 43): the 'ee' family by the standard pattern; L18 ends with writing a short sentence and illustrating an 'ee' word.
- L19 Responding to Reading (PDF p.45): describing an animal the pupil has seen or wants to see.
- L20 Identifying Locations in the Classroom (PDF p.47): using prepositions to say where objects are in the classroom.

**Lessons 21–25 — describing words (theme: Reading and Describing Words).**
- L21 Story: All in One Day (PDF p.49): reading and discussing the poem-text "All in One Day"; it returns in L25 Tracking Poem (PDF p.57), where pupils track words and lines with a pointer as they read.
- L22 Adjectives: Describing People and Places (PDF p.51): describing people, places, animals and objects.
- L23–L24 Word Family: 'eep' (PDF pp.53, 55): standard pattern.

**Lessons 26–30 — punctuation (theme: Reading, Writing and Punctuation).**
- L26 Predictions: Titles and Illustrations (PDF p.59) and L27 Reading for Information (PDF p.61): predicting what the poem "So Many Things to See and Do" will be about from its title and illustrations, then reading and discussing it. The occupation drawings made for this poem (baker, teacher, writer, fisherman) are reused in L33.
- L28 Word Work 1: Changing Verbs to Nouns with 'er' (PDF p.63) and L29 Word Work 2: 'er' (PDF p.65): adding 'r'/'er' to verbs to make nouns (the teach→teacher type), then illustrating and labelling 'er' words.
- L30 Reading Aloud with Punctuation Marks (PDF p.67): reading the poem "Sara Goes to School" aloud, observing commas, full stops and question marks.

**Lessons 31–35 — occupations (theme: Reading and Occupations).**
- L31–L32 Poem: Fruit Treats (PDF pp.69, 71): reading and discussing the poem; L32 uses it to categorise foods (fruits, grains, animal products).
- L33 Talk about Parents and Occupations (PDF p.73): naming and describing parents' occupations.
- L34–L35 Word Family: 'eat' (PDF pp.75, 77): standard pattern.

**Lessons 36–40 — verbs (theme: Reading, Writing and Verbs).**
- L36 The Dog and the Shadow (PDF p.79) and L37 Story: Questions and Answers (PDF p.81): the fable first met in L5 is now read properly (Part 1 in L36) and questioned in L37.
- L38 Word Family: 'oss' (PDF p.83).
- L39 Verbs: To Be, To Go (PDF p.85) and L40 Irregular Verbs: 'to be', 'to go' (PDF p.87): using and writing the two verbs, then using them to talk about the past.

**Lessons 41–45 — drinks and retelling (theme: Reading and Writing).**
- L41 Tiny Tale: A Sweet Drink (PDF p.89) and L42 Reading and Tracking Text (PDF p.91): co-writing the "Sweet Drinks" Tiny Tale, then tracking and reading it independently.
- L43 List of Favourite Drinks (PDF p.93): writing a list.
- L44 Retelling The Dog and the Shadow (PDF p.95): the fable's third appearance — retelling it whole.
- L45 Word Family: 'ink' (PDF p.97).

**Lessons 46–50 — present tense and number words (theme: Reading, Present Tense and Number Words).**
- L46 Poem: Big Buzzing Bees (PDF p.99) and L47 Big Buzzing Bumble Bee (cont.) (PDF p.101): reading, tracking and discussing the poem; writing a sentence and drawing a picture about it. The poem returns in L55.
- L48 Word Family: 'ick' (PDF p.103).
- L49 Present Tense: Morning Activities (PDF p.105): describing what pupils do every morning before school.
- L50 Number Words (PDF p.107): reading and writing number words 0 to 20, with the rhyme "Count and Eat".

**Lessons 51–55 — questions (theme: Reading, Writing and Questions).**
- L51 Poem 'Zippy Zippers' (PDF p.109): reading a poem with rhythm and clapping; L52 Reading Words (PDF p.111) picks 2-syllable words out of it; L53 Answering 'W' Questions (PDF p.113) writes simple questions with what, where and why about it.
- L54 Word Family: 'ip' (PDF p.115).
- L55 Responding to a Poem (PDF p.117): illustrating the "Big Buzzing Bees" poem from L46.

**Lessons 56–60 — closing the term (theme: Reading and Writing / Describing Words).**
- L56 Tiny Tale: 'Before School' (PDF p.119) and L57 Reading a Tiny Tale (PDF p.121): co-writing, then tracking and reading aloud.
- L58 Prefix 'un' (PDF p.122): using 'un' to create new words (from well, able, lock, afraid, lucky, fair, pack). Note the TOC gives this lesson printed p.117, breaking the even-page pattern of the rest of the book. The prefix returns as a review in Term 2, L94.
- L59 Word Family: 'ock' (PDF p.124).
- L60 Writing: Preparing for School (PDF p.126): writing about getting ready for school in the morning, reusing the "Before School" tale.

**Honest negatives for this book.** No pupil book, workbook or exercises separate from the teacher's plan exist in this record — pupil writing happens in exercise books on the plan's instructions. No publication year, ISBN or publisher is printed anywhere in the book. Several embedded texts are credited inside the plans to the RAISES Education Project's "Read and Write with Me! Stories and Poems for Children in Class III" (e.g. the "My Fast Feet" credit line in Term 3 shows the series credit style); where a plan prints no author, none is claimed here. Header numbering in Lessons 1–10 is faulty as detailed above.

**Citation check.** 16 concrete page anchors were checked against the actual PDF text (foreword signature and "2,700 lesson plans" claim, PDF p.3; TOC Lesson 1 entry, p.4; L-03-001 header and "Joy and the Naughty Monkeys", p.7; the story's byline "by Nathaniel Pearce", p.8; headers L-03-005 p.13, L-03-004 p.17, road-safety song lines p.20, L-03-011 p.29, L-03-016 p.39, L-03-021 p.49, L-03-030 "Sara Goes to School" p.67, L-03-036 p.79, L-03-045 p.97, number words "0 to 20" p.107, L-03-058 prefix 'un' p.122, L-03-060 p.126): 16/16 passed.


# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 3 Lesson Plans, Term 2 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover: "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", badges "3 CLASS" and "2 TERM"; Ministry of Education, Science and Technology; "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education"; "NOT FOR SALE". The cover, foreword (Dr. Minkailu Bah) and Introduction page are the same as Term 1 — see the Term 1 section above; they are not repeated here.
- PDF pages: 132. Lessons 61–120, numbered continuously from Term 1; every lesson 35 minutes in the same five-part shape. The PDF's first page carries only the stray printed number "122" in the text layer (the cover itself is an image, as in all six books).
- No publication year, ISBN or publisher is printed in the book.

## The term's lessons, in order

**Lessons 61–65 — questions (theme: Reading, Writing and Questions).**
- L61 Poem: Fishermen Come Home (Part I) (PDF p.8) and L62 (Part II) (PDF p.10): reading and discussing the week's poem about fishermen returning, then reading or reciting it to a partner.
- L63 Questions and Answers: Why, How (PDF p.12) and L64 Who, What, Where, When (PDF p.14): asking and answering questions about the same poem, first why/how, then who/what/where/when with question-word flash cards.
- L65 Word Family 'oat' (PDF p.16): the standard create-read-write word-family lesson (the two-lesson illustrate-and-label follow-up used in Term 1 is not repeated for every family this term; several families get one lesson).

**Lessons 66–70 — a story in depth (theme: Reading and Problems in Stories).**
- L66 Fanta Looks After Baby Lahai (Part I) (PDF p.18) and L67 (Part II) (PDF p.20): reading and discussing the story of Fanta caring for baby Lahai, then answering questions on Part II.
- L68 Reading: Characters (PDF p.22): describing the characters in the story.
- L69 Word Family: 'ook' (PDF p.24).
- L70 Problem in 'Fanta Looks After Baby Lahai' (PDF p.26): discussing the problem Fanta had and how it was resolved — the term's first explicit problem-and-resolution lesson.

**Lessons 71–75 — verbs (theme: Reading, Writing and Verbs).**
- L71 Poem 'Going Home' (PDF p.28): reading and discussing the poem together (new words prepared: happy, dance, inside, heart).
- L72 Word Family 'ance' (PDF p.30).
- L73 Reading and Writing Short Words (PDF p.32): making a list of words and phrases about a time the pupil travelled away from their community, using the story "Fatu Goes to Freetown" printed at the end of the plan.
- L74 Present Progressive Tense (PDF p.34): using '-ing' words to talk about things happening now.
- L75 Reciting a Poem About a Trip (PDF p.36): reciting a short poem to another pupil.

**Lessons 76–80 — verbs and pronouns (theme: Reading, Verbs and Pronouns).**
- L76 Number Words 2 (PDF p.38): number words 20 to 40, with a counting song (Term 1 covered 0–20; L110 covers the full 0–50).
- L77 Writing with the Verb 'To Be' (PDF p.40): using 'to be' to talk and write about pupils' own lives.
- L78 Comparative Adjectives (PDF p.42): listing and using comparative adjectives.
- L79 Possessive Pronouns (PDF p.44): talking about classroom life using possessive pronouns.
- L80 The Wind and the Sun (Part I) (PDF p.46) and L81 (Part II) (PDF p.48): the Aesop-style fable read and discussed in two parts.

**Lessons 82–85 — opinions (theme: Reading, Writing and Opinions).**
- L82 Poem: The Day's End (PDF p.50): tracking and reading the poem aloud; L84 Writing Sentences (PDF p.54) writes a sentence about the end of the day from it.
- L83 Word Family: 'ay' (PDF p.52).
- L85 Expressing an Opinion (PDF p.56): expressing a simple opinion, worked on the poem version of "The Ant and the Grasshopper" — the same text that becomes Term 3's main folktale sequence (Term 3, L126–129).

**Lessons 86–90 — health (theme: Reading, Writing and Communicating Information).**
- L86 Tiny Tale: Staying Healthy (PDF p.58) and L87 Tiny Tale: Vocabulary (PDF p.60): co-writing a Tiny Tale about staying healthy; reading it with a partner, drawing a new word and labelling it with a sentence.
- L88 The Word Family 'ean' (PDF p.62).
- L89 Healthy Poster (PDF p.64) and L90 Giving Information (PDF p.66): designing a poster in pairs telling people how to stay healthy, then writing a sentence for such a poster.

**Lessons 91–95 — punctuation through a folktale (theme: Reading and Writing with Punctuation).**
- L91 Mr. Tortoise Flies (Part I) (PDF p.68) and L92 (Part II) (PDF p.70): reading, discussing and tracking the folktale of the tortoise who flies with the birds.
- L93 Word Family: 'end' (PDF p.72).
- L94 Prefix Review: 'un' (PDF p.74): creating words with the prefix 'un' — a deliberate return to Term 1, L58.
- L95 Retelling the Story 'Mr. Tortoise Flies' (PDF p.76): retelling in three short sentences using capital letters and full stops.

**Lessons 96–100 — reading for information (theme: Reading for Information and Vocabulary).**
- L96 Informational Text: A New Cook-Stove Read Aloud (PDF p.78) and L97 Read Aloud: A New Cook-Stove (PDF p.80): discussing and reading aloud a non-fiction text about an improved cook-stove, then answering questions on it.
- L98 Vocabulary (PDF p.82): writing down and defining new words from the cook-stove text.
- L99 Word Family: 'ice' (PDF p.84) and L100 Word Family 'ice' (PDF p.86): the family lesson, then writing a simple poem using 'ice' words; both lessons work from the poem "Soup on Rice", which returns in L118.

**Lessons 101–105 — vocabulary (theme: Reading and Vocabulary).**
- L101 Word Family 'oon' (PDF p.88), built on the short poem "Moon".
- L102 Poem: Moonlight Music (PDF p.90): reading and performing the poem together; L103 Vocabulary in the 'Moonlight Music' Poem (PDF p.92): writing a list of things seen in the moonlight.
- L104 Synonyms: Introduction (PDF p.94) and L105 Antonyms (Opposite Words): Introduction (PDF p.96): listing synonyms, then opposites, for familiar words.

**Lessons 106–110 — folktales and number words (theme: Reading, Folktales and Number Words).**
- L106 Why Dogs Run After Vehicles (Part I) (PDF p.98) and L107 (Part II) (PDF p.100): reading and discussing the folktale, with printed word definitions; L109 Characters and Setting (PDF p.104) rereads it to explain where it happens and who is in it.
- L108 Word Family: 'ire' (PDF p.102).
- L110 Number Words 3 (PDF p.106): number words 0 to 50 (the plan's second page prints the table "NUMBER WORDS 40-50": forty, forty-one, forty-two…).

**Lessons 111–115 — farming (theme: Reading / Reading and Comparing).**
- L111 Tiny Tale: Farming (PDF p.108): reading the co-written "Farming" Tiny Tale; L113 Asking Questions: Farming (PDF p.112) asks and answers questions about where foods come from, using a printed table "Where Food Comes From" and local foods (rice, cassava…); L114 Reading: 'Farming' Tiny Tale (headed "L_03_114", PDF p.114) reads it aloud in groups of three.
- L112 Word Family: 'ear' (PDF p.110).
- L115 Occupations 2 (PDF p.116): relating the Farming tale to jobs in the community, with pictures of people in different occupations (Term 1's occupation work was L33).

**Lessons 116–120 — harvest (theme: Reading, Writing and Vocabulary).**
- L116 Vocabulary: Harvests and Crops (PDF p.118): describing crops grown and harvested in Sierra Leone, from the poem "Pepper, Pepper" and real local foods (rice, cassava, okra, mango, oranges).
- L117 Poem: Rice Harvest (PDF p.120): reading and discussing the poem, with printed definitions.
- L118 Diagram: A Field of Rice (PDF p.122): drawing and labelling a diagram of a rice farm, reusing the "Soup on Rice" poem from L99.
- L119 Word Family: 'ound' (PDF p.124), worked through the song "Poda Poda".
- L120 Word Family: Writing Sentences (PDF p.126): writing a sentence with a word from the 'ound' family.

**Honest negatives for this book.** Header "L_03_114" is printed with underscores (PDF p.114); a few Term 2 lesson headers (L69, L86–L90) print the Theme line before the Lesson Number, but the numbers are present and correct. No pupil book or separate exercises exist in this record; no year, ISBN or publisher is printed. Where a plan names no author for its story or poem, none is claimed here.

**Citation check.** 15 concrete page anchors were checked against the actual PDF text (foreword "2,700 lesson plans", p.3; L-03-061 "Fishermen Come Home", p.8; L-03-066 Fanta/Baby Lahai, p.18; L-03-074 present progressive '-ing', p.34; L-03-080 "The Wind and the Sun", p.46; "Tiny Tale: Staying Healthy", p.58; L-03-091 "Mr. Tortoise Flies", p.68; L-03-096 "A New Cook-Stove", p.78; L-03-102 "Moonlight Music", p.90; L-03-104 synonyms, p.94; L-03-106 "Why Dogs Run After Vehicles", p.98; number-words table 40–50 with "forty-one", p.107; L-03-115 Occupations 2/Farming, p.116; L-03-117 "Rice Harvest", p.120; L-03-120, p.126): 15/15 passed.


# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 3 Lesson Plans, Term 3 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover: "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", badges "3 CLASS" and "3 TERM"; Ministry of Education, Science and Technology; "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education"; "NOT FOR SALE". Cover, foreword and Introduction page are the same as Terms 1–2 (see Term 1 section).
- PDF pages: 68 — about half the length of Terms 1 and 2. It contains 30 lessons, Lessons 121–150, each 35 minutes in the same five-part shape. The book itself prints no explanation for the shorter term; the foreword's general statement that the plans "will not take the whole term" is the only relevant note, and it appears in all six books.
- No publication year, ISBN or publisher is printed in the book.

## The term's lessons, in order

**Lessons 121–125 — speed (theme: Reading, Writing and Categories / Comparing).**
- L121 Poem: 'My Fast Feet' (PDF p.6): reading the poem together. The text printed at the end of the plan (PDF p.7) is credited to "1, 2, 3 Read and Write with Me! Stories and Poems for Children in Class III" (RAISES Education Project); in my own summary: the speaker watches a slow caterpillar with its many tiny feet and wonders how it keeps them in order, having only two feet itself. New words are underlined for teaching.
- L122 Word Family: 'ast' (PDF p.8): the rime family with flash-card onsets f, c, m, l, p.
- L123 Categories: Fast and Slow Things (PDF p.10): sorting things into fast and slow categories from the poem; L124 Writing Sentences (PDF p.12) writes sentences about fast and slow things.
- L125 Reciting a Poem: 'My Fast Feet' (PDF p.14): reciting with expression — the poem's third use.

**Lessons 126–130 — the ant and the grasshopper (theme: Reading, Writing and Comparing).**
- L126 Story: 'The Ant and the Grasshopper' (PDF p.16) and L127 Folktale: 'The Ant and the Grasshopper' (PDF p.18): reading the text (the plans head the printed text "POEM THE ANT AND THE GRASSHOPPER"), then dramatising it. This is the same text used for opinion work in Term 2, L85; here it anchors a full week.
- L128 Word Family: 'ine' (PDF p.20).
- L129 Comparing: Grasshoppers and Ants (PDF p.22): describing and comparing the two creatures from the text.
- L130 Verb 'To Be': Present Progressive (PDF p.24): 'to be' + '-ing' to describe present actions (returning to Term 2, L74).

**Lessons 131–135 — non-fiction (theme: Nonfiction Reading).**
- L131 Prepare for Reading 'The Pygmy Hippopotamus' (PDF p.26): listing what pupils know and want to know about the pygmy hippopotamus (a know/want-to-know chart).
- L132 Informational Text: 'The Pygmy Hippopotamus' (PDF p.28): reading the non-fiction text; L134 Answering Simple Questions (PDF p.32) asks and answers questions on it; L135 Reading to Learn (PDF p.34) lists what was learnt from it. The text is credited in the plans to the RAISES Education Project as an abridged text.
- L133 Word Family: 'ix' (PDF p.30).

**Lessons 136–140 — colours and the past tense (theme: Reading, Colours and Verbs).**
- L136 Story: 'The Rabbit and the Tortoise' (PDF p.36): reading the race fable together; L137 Story: Lesson or Moral (PDF p.38) rereads it aloud and discusses the lesson it teaches. The story returns in L143.
- L138 Word Family: 'est' (PDF p.40).
- L139 Colours of the Flag (PDF p.42): identifying and describing the colours of the national flag.
- L140 The Simple Past Tense (PDF p.43 — a one-lesson offset: this plan starts on an odd printed page): using the simple past to describe a week at school.

**Lessons 141–145 — errands (theme: Reading, Verbs and Punctuation).**
- L141 Story: 'Mamoud Goes to Yara' (PDF p.45): reading and discussing the story; L142 Vocabulary: Errands (PDF p.47) uses different verbs to name and talk about errands pupils run; L144 Vocabulary: 'Mamoud Goes to Yara' (PDF p.51) defines key words from the story.
- L143 Fluent Reading: Punctuation Marks (PDF p.49): using commas, full stops and question marks to read fluently, worked on "The Rabbit and the Tortoise" from L136.
- L145 Expressing Opinions (PDF p.53): expressing opinions about issues relating to the pupil's family.

**Lessons 146–150 — animals and main idea (theme: Reading, Nonfiction and Writing).**
- L146 Colours: Review (PDF p.55): grouping objects by colour.
- L147 Informational Text: 'Little Flying Flowers' (PDF p.56): reading the text (headed as a poem in the plan) about butterflies; L150 Reading: Main Idea (headed L-03-150, PDF p.62) names and explains its main idea.
- L148 Vocabulary: Similar and Different (PDF p.58): comparing different objects and animals.
- L149 Related Sentences: Why I Appreciate Animals (headed "L_03_149", PDF p.60): writing sentences about animals and why they are important in our lives.

**Honest negatives for this book.** Term 3 has 30 lessons against 60 in each of Terms 1 and 2, and the book gives no reason for the difference. Two header numbers are printed with underscores or displaced layout ("L_03_149"; L150's title and number are swapped in the header block). No pupil book exists in this record; no year, ISBN or publisher is printed.

**Citation check.** 13 concrete page anchors were checked against the actual PDF text (foreword's "Accelerated Teaching Syllabi" / "August 2015", p.3; L-03-121 'My Fast Feet', p.6; the poem's RAISES "Read and Write with Me" credit and caterpillar content, p.7; L-03-126 Ant and Grasshopper, p.16; L-03-132 Pygmy Hippopotamus, p.28; L-03-136 Rabbit and Tortoise, p.36; L-03-139 Colours of the Flag, p.42; L-03-140 Simple Past Tense, p.43; L-03-141 Mamoud Goes to Yara, p.45; L-03-145 Expressing Opinions, p.53; L-03-147 Little Flying Flowers, p.56; "L_03_149" Why I Appreciate Animals, p.60; L-03-150 Reading: Main Idea, p.62): 13/13 passed.


# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 4 Lesson Plans, Term 1 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover: "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", badges "4 CLASS" and "1 TERM"; Ministry of Education, Science and Technology; "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education"; "NOT FOR SALE". Cover, foreword (Dr. Minkailu Bah) and Introduction page are the same as the Primary 3 books (see the Primary 3 Term 1 section).
- PDF pages: 132. Lessons 1–60 for Primary 4 (numbering restarts at 1 for the new class; lesson codes are L-04-001 to L-04-060), each 35 minutes in the same five-part shape as Primary 3: Opening, Introduction to the New Material, Guided Practice, Independent Practice, Closing.
- Difference from Primary 3: the work is organised by grammar and comprehension themes rather than word families — there are no rime word-family lessons in Primary 4 — and several embedded stories are credited to a named author, Karen Waterston, with others adapted from ESSPIN lesson plans (named in the credit lines as ESSPIN Literacy, Class 4). No publication year, ISBN or publisher is printed in the book.
- Header numbers and the Table of Contents agree throughout this book (checked lesson by lesson).

## The term's lessons, in order

**Lessons 1–5 — main points and questions (theme: Reading Comprehension).** All five lessons work on one story, "Teteh and his hoop" (named in the aids from L2 on; it is the unnamed "story at the end of the lesson plan" in L1). In my own summary: Teteh's hoop escapes down a hill and a chase of family members and villagers ends when his long-legged older sister catches it and everyone crashes into her.
- L1 Main Points of a Story (PDF p.8): identifying the main events of the story. L2 Main Points of a Story: Practice (PDF p.10): retelling with a focus on main points.
- L3 Questions about a Story: Who? What? (PDF p.12), L4 Where? When? (PDF p.14), L5 Why? How? (PDF p.16): learning to ask each pair of question words about the story.

**Lessons 6–10 — storytelling (theme: Storytelling).**
- L6 Storytelling (PDF p.18): composing an original story from beginning to end; the class story is made into a book about "My friend".
- L7 Characters (PDF p.20): describing major and minor characters in the pupils' own story. L8 Character's Challenges (PDF p.22): identifying problems a character faces and how to resolve them.
- L9 Setting (PDF p.24) and L10 Plot (PDF p.26): describing the setting, and retelling the main events — both worked on the pupils' own stories and the "My friend" book made in L6.

**Lessons 11–15 — structure (theme: Structure and Punctuation).**
- L11 Verbs (PDF p.28): using different verbs in a paragraph describing a scene.
- L12 Difficult Spellings (PDF p.30): writing out, categorising and revising difficult spellings from a table.
- L13 Punctuation (PDF p.32): punctuation and capital letters correctly in an original composition.
- L14 Nouns and Pronouns (PDF p.34): sentences using nouns and pronouns, from example and practice sentences.
- L15 Punctuation: Colon and Semi-colon (PDF p.36): comparing the use of the colon and semi-colon.

**Lessons 16–20 — nouns and verbs in a passage (theme: Nouns and Verbs).**
- L16 Nouns (PDF p.38): creating categories of nouns (people, animals, places, things).
- L17 Reading: Pronouns (PDF p.40), L18 Reading: Nouns (PDF p.42), L19 Reading: Verbs (PDF p.44): selecting pronouns, types of nouns, and action verbs from a passage. L18's passage is the story "My Mother and Her Motorbike" by Karen Waterston (printed at the end of the plan): in my summary, mother picks up passenger after passenger — sister, teacher, farmer, nurse with baby — until the overloaded bike goes very slowly; the same story serves L19 and L20.
- L20 Categories of Nouns (PDF p.46): examining noun categories in the passage.

**Lessons 21–25 — letters and invitations (theme: Writing Letters and Invitations).**
- L21 Letter Writing (PDF p.48): listing and defining the parts of a letter, from an example letter.
- L22 Oral Descriptions (PDF p.50): retelling the details of an event such as a birthday party or sports event, with a short story at the end of the plan.
- L23 Letter Writing (PDF p.52): writing a short informal letter.
- L24 Writing an Invitation (PDF p.54): writing an invitation to a friend.
- L25 People and Trades (PDF p.56): explaining trades and occupations in the community, through multiple-choice sentences.

**Lessons 26–30 — plurals (theme: Nouns: Singular and Plural).** All five lessons work from one story, "The Blunt Knife" by Karen Waterston (printed at the end of the L26 plan): in my summary, a knife that cannot cut anything travels to a knife-sharpener on a church roof, is sharpened, and comes home cutting everything in sight, to the people's dismay.
- L26 Plural Nouns: -es (PDF p.58), L27 Plural Nouns: -ies (PDF p.60: nouns ending in 'y' → 'ies'), L28 Plural Nouns: -ves (PDF p.62), L29 Singular and Plural Nouns (PDF p.64): classifying singulars and plurals from the passage.
- L30 Retelling (PDF p.66): retelling the plot of the oral story.

**Lessons 31–35 — oral language (theme: Oral Language).**
- L31 Poetry (PDF p.68), L33 Poetry (PDF p.72) and L34 Recitation (PDF p.74) all use one poem, about a girl called Bunty who hates washing, dreams that germs chase her and the Soap King's bubble army rescues her, and scrubs clean afterwards (summarised, not quoted). L31: recite the poem and discuss its moral (the lesson opens by defining germs as tiny bugs that make you ill). L33: defining lines, stanzas and verses. L34: reading poems aloud taking note of punctuation.
- L32 Singing (PDF p.70): singing and discussing the song "We Should Share" by Tanya Cotter (credit line as printed), a sharing song to a familiar tune.
- L35 Dictation (PDF p.76): writing sentences given by dictation.

**Lessons 36–40 — writing skills (theme: Writing Skills).**
- L36 Punctuation (PDF p.78) and L39 Punctuation (PDF p.84): using punctuation marks in original sentences; using them to read sentences fluently (L39 works on a dialogue).
- L37 Spellings (PDF p.80): spelling difficult words from a list.
- L38 Writing (PDF p.82): writing descriptive sentences (the lesson's aid is something green, e.g. a leaf).
- L40 Pronouns (PDF p.86): using pronouns in an original passage.

**Lessons 41–45 — parts of speech.**
- L41 Pronouns (PDF p.88): making sentences with pronouns, using jumbled sentences.
- L42 Verb Tenses in a Passage (PDF p.90): identifying different verb tenses in a passage (the passage is a letter). L43 Verb Tenses in our Sentences (PDF p.92): writing sentences in past, present and future tenses. L44 Verb Tense and Pronouns (PDF p.94): matching pronouns to verb tense, from two tables.
- L45 Continuous Tense (PDF p.96): communicating orally and in writing in the present continuous tense.

**Lessons 46–50 — comparing characters (theme: Comparing Characters).**
- L46 Characters: Actions (PDF p.98) and L47 Characters: Changes (PDF p.100): comparing one character's actions with another's, and examining how a character changes through the story — both worked on the story "Helping Mother" by Karen Waterston (scene 1: a family at home, nobody helping mother, who finally shouts that she will go to the market herself).
- L48 Drama: Market Scenes (PDF p.102): acting a scene with dialogue with a partner. L49 Drama (PDF p.104): dramatising a familiar story.
- L50 Subject–Verb Agreement (PDF p.106): explaining subject-verb agreement, from three sets of sentences.

**Lessons 51–55 — subject-verb agreement.**
- L51 Subject-Verb Agreement in Reported Speech (PDF p.108): reported speech in sentences with subject-verb agreement.
- L52 Subject of a Sentence (PDF p.110): matching subjects and verbs to sentence frames. L53 Verbs in Writing (PDF p.112): using verbs that agree with the subject when writing, from a passage with answers.
- L54 Person (1st, 2nd, 3rd) (PDF p.114): showing how first, second and third person agree with the verb.
- L55 Retell Passages (PDF p.116): retelling a passage read.

**Lessons 56–60 — writing and verbs (theme: Writing and Verbs).**
- L56 Reading Enjoyment (PDF p.118): reading a grade-level text out with expression. The text is the story "Why Snails Move Slowly" (credit line: adapted from ESSPIN lesson plans, Literacy Class 4, Week 16): in my summary, a snail gets stuck in an old woman's porridge in a drought, is put outside still coated, and snails have moved slowly, leaving a porridge-like trail, ever since.
- L57 Lexical/Main Verbs (PDF p.120): identifying and using lexical/main verbs.
- L58 Writing Descriptions (PDF p.122): writing a simple descriptive paragraph (worked from a picture of a window).
- L59 Auxiliary/Helping Verbs (PDF p.124): identifying and using auxiliary/helping verbs through sentence frames.
- L60 Parts of a Story (PDF p.126): telling a story from beginning, middle and end in simple sentences — reusing "Why Snails Move Slowly" from L56, with a picture of a snail.

**Honest negatives for this book.** Many Primary 4 plans refer to their text only as "story/poem at the end of the lesson plan"; where the printed text carries no title or byline in the plan, none is claimed here (the Bunty poem's title, for example, is not printed — the plan calls it only "a poem about Bunty and germs"). No pupil book exists in this record; no year, ISBN or publisher is printed.

**Citation check.** 14 concrete page anchors were checked against the actual PDF text (foreword signature, p.3; L-04-001 Main Points/Reading Comprehension, p.8; the Teteh hoop-chase story text, p.9; L-04-015 colon and semi-colon, p.36; "My Mother and Her Motorbike" by Karen Waterston, p.43; L-04-021 parts of a letter, p.48; "The Blunt Knife" by Karen Waterston, p.59; L-04-031 with Bunty/germs, p.68; "We Should Share" by Tanya Cotter, p.71; L-04-042 verb tenses, p.90; "Helping Mother" by Karen Waterston, p.99; L-04-050 subject–verb agreement, p.106; "Why Snails Move Slowly"/ESSPIN credit, p.119; L-04-060 beginning-middle-end outcome, p.126): 14/14 passed.


# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 4 Lesson Plans, Term 2 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover: "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", badges "4 CLASS" and "2 TERM"; Ministry of Education, Science and Technology; "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education"; "NOT FOR SALE". Cover, foreword and Introduction page are the same as Term 1 (see the Primary 4 Term 1 section).
- PDF pages: 132. Lessons 61–120, numbered continuously from Term 1; lesson codes L-04-061 to L-04-120; every lesson 35 minutes in the same five-part shape. Header numbers and the Table of Contents agree throughout.
- One layout note: L85 (Prefixes and Suffixes) runs long — the TOC places L86 at printed p.53, an odd page, the only break in the even-page lesson starts this term.
- No publication year, ISBN or publisher is printed in the book. Several embedded texts are again adapted from ESSPIN lesson plans where the credit line says so.

## The term's lessons, in order

**Lessons 61–65 — expressing opinions (theme: Expressing Opinions).** The block is built on community cleanliness.
- L61 Answering 'Why' Questions (PDF p.8): answering 'why' questions about the story "Decision" by inferring — some answers are in the story, some must be inferred from what pupils know.
- L62 Personal Attitudes (PDF p.10): expressing an opinion about issues in the community, worked on the play "The village meeting".
- L63 Composition (PDF p.12): drafting a paragraph expressing an opinion, from the text "Rubbish".
- L64 Vocabulary Work (PDF p.14): comparing synonyms and antonyms to work out the meaning of new words, from the text "Let's clean up!".
- L65 Synonyms (PDF p.16): finding words similar in meaning in sentences drawn from the story "Decision" (L61's text).

**Lessons 66–70 — storytelling.**
- L66 Storytelling (PDF p.18): retelling the beginning, middle and end of the traditional story "How Tortoise got his crooked shell" in the pupils' own words.
- L67 Describing an Event (PDF p.20): describing a festival in the pupils' communities.
- L68 Describing an Advertisement (PDF p.22): explaining the purpose of a familiar advertisement or saying, from the text "School picnic".
- L69 Identifying the Main Character in a Traditional Story (PDF p.24) and L70 Analysing Problems (PDF p.26): describing the problem the main character faces in "Grasshopper and Toad – Part 1", then explaining how the character solves the problem and writing an alternative ending (Parts 1 and 2).

**Lessons 71–75 — writing with conventions.**
- L71 Problem Solving involving the Main Character (PDF p.28), L73 Describing how Problems Happen in the Story (PDF p.32) and L75 Decision Making (PDF p.36) all work on the story "Abu's problem" (Part 1): finding ways to solve the main character's problems, describing how the problems happen, and identifying and discussing the decisions the character makes.
- L72 Parts of a Sentence (PDF p.30): identifying the parts of a sentence.
- L74 Description of a Character (PDF p.34): describing a character, from a printed "Character description" passage, applied to a person in the pupil's community.

**Lessons 76–80 — letters (theme: Letter Writing).**
- L76 Compound Sentences (PDF p.38): joining two simple sentences into one compound sentence.
- L77 Language to Persuade (PDF p.40): using specific language to persuade, from the dialogue "Let's go to the river".
- L78 Writing an Informal Letter (PDF p.42) and L79 (PDF p.44): writing an informal letter to a friend persuading them to join the writer at an event, modelled on the letter "A football match".
- L80 Reading Non-Fiction (PDF p.46): using headings and titles to learn from a non-fiction text — the passage "Football around the world".

**Lessons 81–85 — letters continued.**
- L81 To Gain Personal Experience about World Issues (PDF p.48) and L82 Compose a Paragraph about a Single Topic or Idea (PDF p.50): giving personal experience of things happening in the community, then composing a paragraph about those experiences and the pupil's opinion of them, worked on the letter "A school competition".
- L83 Business Letter (PDF p.52): writing a letter to an official in the community.
- L84 Tables of Contents (PDF p.54): using a book's table of contents to find answers to questions.
- L85 Prefixes and Suffixes (PDF p.56): matching words with prefixes and suffixes to create new words.

**Lessons 86–90 — past tense and gender (theme: Grammar/Structure).**
- L86 Regular Past Tense (PDF p.59): changing simple present to past for regular verbs. L87 Irregular Past Tense (PDF p.61): the same for irregular verbs (taught with a ball game and a verb list). L88 Past Tense (PDF p.63): using 'used to' to express the past, from the dialogue "I used to work hard".
- L89 Gender (PDF p.65) and L90 Pronouns and Gender (PDF p.67): identifying nouns by gender for persons and animals (L89 works on the story "Grandmother"), then categorising words and pronouns by gender.

**Lessons 91–95 — original poetry (theme: Original Poetry).**
- L91 Infinitives (PDF p.69): identifying infinitives in a short text. L92 The Use of Infinitives in Writing Composition (PDF p.71): writing original sentences with infinitives.
- L93 Opinion about a Text (PDF p.73): writing a personal opinion about a short passage.
- L94 Poems about our Communities (PDF p.75) and L95 (PDF p.77): composing a short poem about an issue in the community, then reciting an original poem (with chart paper and markers where possible).

**Lessons 96–100 — questions and moral lessons (theme: Asking Questions about a Text; Moral Lessons).** From here the plans print title, theme, class and time inside the title line — a layout variant, with numbers still correct.
- L96 Using 'Who' and 'What' Questions (PDF p.78) and L97 Using 'Why' and 'How' Questions (PDF p.80): asking and answering each question pair to discuss a story or passage.
- L98 Book Talks (PDF p.82): making connections to other stories, with chart paper for group work.
- L99 Moral Lesson: Story (PDF p.84): explaining the moral lesson from a story. L100 Moral Lesson: Poem (PDF p.86): explaining the moral lesson from a poem (aids include a picture of a tortoise and a rabbit).

**Lessons 101–105 — verb tenses (theme: Structure: Verb Tenses).**
- L101 Recitation of Poems (PDF p.88): reciting poems with fluency.
- L102 Verbs in Writing (PDF p.90): using simple present and present continuous tenses in writing. L103 Verbs in Writing (PDF p.92): changing present-tense verbs to past (regular and irregular) in sentences. L104 Categories of Verbs (PDF p.94): arranging verbs into tense categories, from a story.
- L105 Comparing Non-fiction and Fiction (PDF p.96): explaining the difference between fictional stories and non-fiction, from an information text and board drawings.

**Lessons 106–110 — adjectives (theme: Possessive Adjectives and Adjective Phrases).**
- L106 Adjective Review (PDF p.98): describing objects, people and places using adjectives.
- L107 Main Points and Details (PDF p.100): identifying the main idea and supporting details of a non-fiction passage (the plan uses an information text and a drawing of an elephant).
- L108 Identifying Verbs (PDF p.102): identifying various verb tenses in a story passage.
- L109 Possessive Adjectives (PDF p.104): listing possessive adjectives and using them in sentences. L110 Adjective Phrases (PDF p.106): using adjectives in phrases.

**Lessons 111–115 — quantifiers begin (theme: Structure: Quantifiers).**
- L111 Articles: A, An (PDF p.108): the rules for articles, used in sentences.
- L112 Writing a Semi-Formal Letter (PDF p.110) and L113 (Continued) (PDF p.112): writing a semi-formal letter to a specific person, from a model letter and sentences.
- L114 Verb Tenses: Future (PDF p.114): constructing sentences in the future tense.
- L115 Quantifiers: Definition and Examples (PDF p.116): defining and listing quantifiers, from a paragraph.

**Lessons 116–120 — countable nouns, similes, adverbs (theme: Grammar/Structure: Countable and Uncountable Nouns; Similes, Adjectives and Adverbs).**
- L116 Countable and Uncountable Nouns (PDF p.118): classifying countable and uncountable nouns (taught with a dialogue; a glass of water is the optional aid).
- L117 Quantifiers with Countable and Uncountable Nouns (PDF p.120): matching quantifiers to each noun type, from printed questions.
- L118 Identifying Similes in Poems (PDF p.122): defining similes and identifying them in a poem (with a picture of a girl).
- L119 Changing Adjectives to Adverbs (PDF p.124): matching adjectives to adverbs. L120 Creating Adverbs (PDF p.126): creating adverbs from adjectives.

**Honest negatives for this book.** L83's teaching-aids cell is empty in the plan as printed, and a few other aids cells (L86, L119) are blank in the text layer where the content sits in the preparation column instead; the lessons themselves are complete. No pupil book exists in this record; no year, ISBN or publisher is printed. Unnamed poems and stories are described, not given invented titles.

**Citation check.** 13 concrete page anchors were checked against the actual PDF text (L-04-061 with the story "Decision", p.8; L-04-066 "How Tortoise got his crooked shell", p.18; L-04-075 Abu/decisions outcome, p.36; L-04-080 "Football around the world", p.46; L-04-085 prefixes and suffixes, p.56; L-04-089 gender for persons and animals, p.65; L-04-096 'who'/'what' questions, p.78; L-04-101 recitation of poems, p.88; L-04-107 main points and details with elephant drawing, p.100; L-04-111 Articles: A, An, p.108; L-04-115 quantifiers, p.116; L-04-118 similes, p.122; L-04-120 creating adverbs, p.126): 13/13 passed.


# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 4 Lesson Plans, Term 3 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover: "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", badges "4 CLASS" and "3 TERM"; Ministry of Education, Science and Technology; "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education"; "NOT FOR SALE". Cover, foreword and Introduction page are the same as Terms 1–2 (see the Primary 4 Term 1 section).
- PDF pages: 68 — half the length of Terms 1 and 2, containing 30 lessons, Lessons 121–150, each 35 minutes in the same five-part shape. As in Primary 3 Term 3, the book prints no explanation for the shorter term.
- Lesson codes L-04-121 to L-04-150; header numbers and the Table of Contents agree throughout. Several plans credit their source texts to ESSPIN lesson plans (e.g. "ESSPIN lesson plans, Primary Level 4" appears in credit lines this term).
- No publication year, ISBN or publisher is printed in the book.

## The term's lessons, in order

**Lessons 121–125 — reported speech and word parts (theme: Grammar/Structure: Reported Speech, Prefixes, Suffixes, Word parts).**
- L121 Reported Speech (PDF p.7) and L122 Reported Speech (PDF p.9): using reported speech in role play, then constructing complex sentences with reported speech. Both work on the story "Lost in the forest" — the L121 plan walks pupils through converting direct speech step by step (a character's "I can hear voices" becomes "she could hear voices"; "It's my mother and father" becomes "it was her mother and father").
- L123 Idioms and Proverbs (PDF p.11): explaining the meaning of idioms or proverbs, from the story "A friend in need".
- L124 Respond with Evidence (PDF p.13): using evidence from the text to support a response — again on "A friend in need".
- L125 Word Parts (PDF p.15): dividing words into parts for meaning (prefixes, suffixes, roots).

**Lessons 126–130 — writing a scene (theme: Writing a Scene).** The block is built on one story, "My adventurous aunt" (also spelled "My adventourous aunt" in one plan heading — sic).
- L126 Describe a Family Member (PDF p.17): describing and writing about a person in the pupil's family, modelled on the story.
- L127 Story Writing (PDF p.19): developing a character in a specific setting. L128 Story Writing (Continued) (PDF p.21): writing a short scene with that character, continuing the class story begun in L127 from a paragraph of the aunt story.
- L129 Prepositions (PDF p.23): comparing and contrasting different prepositions (taught physically, with a book and two bags).
- L130 Conditional Statements with 'If' (PDF p.25): constructing conditional statements with 'if', from a paragraph of the aunt story.

**Lessons 131–135 — following instructions (theme: Following Instructions).**
- L131 Imperative Verb Tense (PDF p.27): explaining and writing imperatives for giving instructions, taken from the recipe "Plantain Mosa" printed at the end of the plan.
- L132 Connectives (PDF p.29): identifying and listing connectives that show transition, from the same recipe. L133 Connectives (PDF p.31): matching connectives to sentences in a passage.
- L134 Original Sentences (PDF p.33): writing sentences using connectives when giving out instructions.
- L135 Following Oral Instructions (PDF p.35): following several oral instructions; the written work is starting a letter, with a model letter and printed instructions for starting one.

**Lessons 136–140 — emerging issues (theme: Emerging Issues).**
- L136 Important Issues (PDF p.37): naming issues important to the pupil's family, community and Sierra Leone; the lesson's text is the poem "Brothers and Sisters".
- L137 Language of Agreement (PDF p.39): communicating agreement or disagreement with another's opinion, using speech-bubble frames.
- L138 Cause (and Effect) (PDF p.41): listing the causes of problems in the community. L139 (Cause and) Effect (PDF p.43): discussing the effects of those problems.
- L140 Opinions about Issues (PDF p.45): expressing an original thought about an important issue; the lesson's text is "HIV and AIDS", with questions at the end of the plan.

**Lessons 141–145 — issues and problems in the community.**
- L141 Sentences (PDF p.47): using simple and compound sentences to communicate an idea.
- L142 Developing Solutions (PDF p.49): explaining and writing about a solution to a community issue or problem, using a paragraph outline (the lesson opens with a picture of an elephant with tusks).
- L143 Paragraph Writing (PDF p.51): writing a paragraph — a group of sentences about a single topic — worked on the text "Poaching must be stopped".
- L144 Paragraph Reading (PDF p.53): responding to the main idea of a paragraph about elephants, with printed questions.
- L145 Proofread for Punctuation (PDF p.55): rereading sentences to add and edit punctuation, from sentences printed with incorrect punctuation.

**Lessons 146–150 — reading fluently (theme: Reading Fluently).** The whole final block works on one story, "The boy who cried wolf".
- L146 Context Clues (PDF p.57): determining the meaning of words from context, oral and written, in the story.
- L147 Reading with Punctuation Clues (PDF p.59): reading a passage from the story, pausing and stopping for punctuation marks.
- L148 Reading Phrases (PDF p.61): grouping related words into phrases for fluent reading, on the story's first paragraph.
- L149 Reading with Expression (PDF p.63): dramatising a short passage with expression.
- L150 Write Complex Sentences (PDF p.65): writing complex sentences about a single idea, using a sentence frame from the preparation — the last lesson of the Primary 4 year.

**Honest negatives for this book.** Term 3 has 30 lessons against 60 in each of Terms 1 and 2, unexplained in the book. The story title is spelled two ways in the plans ("My adventurous aunt" / "My adventourous aunt"). No pupil book exists in this record; no year, ISBN or publisher is printed.

**Citation check.** 14 concrete page anchors were checked against the actual PDF text (L-04-121 reported speech with "Lost in the forest", p.7; L-04-123 idioms and proverbs with "A friend in need", p.11; L-04-124 respond with evidence, p.13; L-04-126 "My adventurous aunt", p.17; L-04-130 conditional statements with 'if', p.25; L-04-131 "Plantain Mosa"/imperatives, p.27; L-04-132 connectives with the recipe, p.29; L-04-136 "Brothers and Sisters", p.37; L-04-140 "HIV and AIDS", p.45; L-04-141 simple and compound sentences, p.47; L-04-143 "Poaching must be stopped", p.51; L-04-146 context clues with "The boy who cried wolf", p.57; L-04-148 reading phrases, p.61; L-04-150 write complex sentences, p.65): 14/14 passed.

# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 5 Lesson Plans, Term 1 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover: "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", with cover badges "5 CLASS" and "1 TERM", and "NOT FOR SALE" at the foot of the cover (PDF p. 1, a cover image — it carries no extractable text).
- Issuing authority as printed on the cover: "Ministry of Education, Science and Technology", shown beside the Sierra Leone coat of arms and "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education".
- Year: no publication year is printed on the cover or in the foreword as read. The foreword is signed "Dr. Minkailu Bah, Minister of Education, Science and Technology" and refers back to the Accelerated Teaching Syllabi of August 2015 and to the Ebola epidemic; it announces "2,700 lesson plans" across primary and JSS Language Arts and Mathematics. The same foreword and the same one-page "Introduction to the Lesson Plan Manual" are repeated, word for word in substance, in all six books in this set.
- PDF pages: 132. PDF p. 2 is blank. Printed pagination runs from printed p. 1 (the Introduction) and printed p. 2 (Lesson 1); in this book Lesson 1 starts on PDF p. 8.
- Form: this is a TEACHER book, not a pupil book. Sierra Leone's primary Language Arts record in this set is lesson plans only; there is no primary pupil book. Every lesson is a scripted 35-minute plan with the same skeleton — Lesson Title / Theme / Lesson Number / Class 5 / Time, Learning Outcomes, Teaching Aids, Preparation, then Opening, Introduction to the New Material, Guided Practice, Independent Practice and Closing. The texts pupils read are printed at the end of each lesson plan, for the teacher to write on the board; pupils work in exercise books. There are no week headings anywhere: the term is one numbered run, Lessons 1–60 (L-05-001 to L-05-060), two printed pages per lesson in this book.

## Lesson map — Term 1, Lessons 1–60, in order

- Lessons 1–5, Theme: Reading Comprehension. L1 Talking About Text and L2 Using Illustrations both work on one short board story, "The Greedy Camel" (Ali lets his camel into his small tent bit by bit on a cold night and ends up outside himself — summarised, not reproduced): sequencing sentences, using the two board pictures (beginning/end), and the vocabulary pair greedy/generous. L3 Synonyms and Antonyms introduces the two terms off that story; L4 Synonyms has pupils compose sentences using synonyms; L5 Antonyms has them write sentences using antonyms.
- Lessons 6–10, Theme: Structure in Speaking. L6 Reported Speech (telling someone what another person said, worked through a "crack in the cupboard" mystery picture); L7 Making Polite Requests (classroom requests, with classroom objects as vocabulary); L8 Word Parts and L9–L10 New Vocabulary build compound nouns (football, waterfall, rainbow type words; combining word parts to make new compounds; a word-square; using new words in sentences that show their meaning).
- Lessons 11–15, Theme: Literature. The story elements are named and drilled — characters, setting, plot — first on the Greedy Camel story (L11), then comparing characters (L12), comparing the plots of two stories (L13), differentiating settings inside one story through the senses (L14), and L15 Monitor Understanding, rereading the story of Amina / "the Old Woman and the Corn" to explore its characters.
- Lessons 16–20, Theme: Reading Skills. L16 Story Telling (planning a story with a character pyramid: character, setting, plot); L17 Writing a Story from that plan; L18 Retelling Stories (pupils share their own stories); L19 Fiction (talking about favourite books/stories that are not true); L20 Nonfiction Text (a report — information that is true — set against fiction).
- Lessons 21–25, Theme: Comparing and Contrasting. L21 adjectives and adverbs and the language of comparatives ("comparatives" is the book's word) for comparing two people; L22 the same language applied in Science (TOC title: Living and Non-Living Things; the lesson as taught compares animals); L23 applied in Math (fractions ⅓, ¼, ½ against whole numbers); L24 Reading for Information (a bicycle — labelling its parts from an information text); L25 Paragraph Writing (comparing the teacher's two sisters, Esther and Grace, and writing in paragraphs).
- Lessons 26–30, Theme: Creative Expression. L26 Reading a Poem (a poem in two stanzas of ten lines each; "stanza" is taught); L27 Describing Emotions (emotion adjectives — happy, scared, angry, excited, sad, annoyed); L28 Writing About Events (party, wedding, football match, school dance as examples); L29 Garrie Processing (a process text: pupils learn the process verbs — peel, grate, crush, drain, package and others — and order the steps of making garrie from cassava); L30 People in Your Community (reading an information text about a man named Emmerson into a table, then planning and writing a paragraph about an important person in the pupil's own community).
- Lessons 31–34, Theme: Vocabulary Development. L31 Reading a Poem — the poem "Windy Days" (three stanzas, counted and discussed); L32 Oral African Stories — how Anansi the spider got his thin legs (told orally from two board pictures, fat-legged and thin-legged Anansi); L33 Vocabulary (word parts again, e.g. bi- in bicycle meaning two); L34 Vocabulary (words and sentences taken from the Anansi story).
- Lessons 35–40, Theme: African Stories / Vocabulary Development (L35). A five-lesson writing cycle on the same character/setting/plot frame: L35 setting, L36 planning a character who lives in that setting, L37 Structure (plot), L38 Role Play (acting the beginning of the story written the day before), L39 Writing African Stories (finishing the story), L40 Reading African Stories (sharing the finished stories with the class).
- Lessons 41–45, Theme: Reading Skills. L41 Speaking (new vocabulary taught ahead of a new story); L42 Oral Reading: Phrases, L43 Oral Reading: Punctuation and L44 Oral Reading: Expression — a three-lesson fluency sequence on one story, "The boy who made wishes come true" (its boy character is Umeh); L45 Role Play of that same story.
- Lessons 46–50, Theme: Creative Expression — "Emerging Issues". L46 Expressing Opinions (school subjects and adjectives for them, in a mind-map); L47 For and Against, L48 Identifying (main characters of the wishes story, on a character pyramid), L49 Expressing and L50 Reading all turn on one text: a letter by a boy named Bintu protesting a proposed new road that would destroy his village park and fields — pupils sort Bintu's case against the businessmen's case for the road, join a for-point and an against-point in one sentence with "although", match paragraph headings (the park, the wildlife, reason for writing, closing, the fields) and answer questions on the letter.
- Lessons 51–55, Theme: Structure in English. L51 Writing Reported Speech and L52 Speaking Reported Speech (reporting what Marie/Omar, then Georgieta/Ma Hassanatou, say); L53 Reading: Personal Experience (a text about Barrie, who was afraid of starting a new school); L54 Reading: Text-Text Connections (more about Barrie, connected back to the earlier text); L55 Vocabulary: Using Context (guessing word meaning from the sentence around it).
- Lessons 56–60, Theme: Writing Skills. L56 Writing: Informal Letters (a letter to a far-away friend, from before phones/internet as the framing); L57 Writing: Semi-Formal Letters (to someone known but not a close friend); L58–L59 Conjunctions and Compound Sentences (joining with and, but, because, or — two lessons, the second headed "Continued"); L60 Question Tags (a different way of asking questions, closing the term).

## What a pupil is actually taught, and how

The pupil never holds this book. What reaches the pupil is: short board texts read aloud and copied, oral pair work, and writing in an exercise book. Across the term a pupil is taught to (a) talk about a story's events and sequence them, (b) use illustrations, (c) handle synonyms, antonyms and compound nouns, (d) use reported speech and polite requests in speech and writing, (e) name and compare character/setting/plot, (f) plan, write, retell and role-play their own stories, including an "African story" cycle, (g) tell fiction from nonfiction and read for information, (h) compare with comparative adjectives/adverbs across English, science and maths topics, (i) read poems by stanza, describe emotions, explain a local food process (garrie) in order, and write about a community figure, (j) argue for/against an "emerging issue" (the road) from a model letter, and (k) write informal and semi-formal letters, join sentences into compound sentences, and form question tags.

## Repeats and continuities

- Reported speech is taught twice in this one term: introduced at L6 (speaking) and returned to, in writing and speaking, at L51–L52.
- Letters appear at L56–L57 here and return as a major strand in Terms 2–3 of both Primary 5 and Primary 6 (see the later sections); question tags (L60) return in Primary 6 Term 1; conjunctions and compound sentences (L58–L59) return in Primary 6 Term 3.

## Honest negatives — this book

- No phonics, letter-sound work, handwriting or dictation anywhere in the book; reading is whole-text oral reading, not decoding instruction.
- No spelling system: spelling is only mentioned as something to "think about" when writing (e.g. L30).
- No test, quiz or exam paper is printed in the book; the Introduction only tells teachers to use spare time "to review material or prepare for exams".
- No pupil-facing text of any length: the longest reading matter is the short end-of-plan board text for each lesson. No novel, chapter book or reader is set.
- The poem in L26 is not named in the parts of the plan read for this filing; "Windy Days" (L31) and the Anansi story (L32) are named. Story and poem texts are summarised in this filing, never transcribed at length.

## Citation check

15 concrete page anchors were checked against the actual PDF text (pdftotext -layout, page by page) and 15/15 passed, including: PDF p. 3 foreword ("Accelerated Teaching Syllabi in August 2015"); PDF p. 4 contents ("Lesson 1: Talking About Text"); PDF p. 9 story heading "THE GREEDY CAMEL"; PDF p. 18 L6 "Reported Speech"; PDF p. 36 "Woman and the Corn"; PDF p. 58 "stanza" (L26); PDF p. 64 "Garrie Processing" (L29); PDF p. 68 "Windy Days" (L31); PDF p. 70 "Anansi" (L32); PDF p. 98 "school subjects" (L46); PDF p. 108 "Writing Reported Speech" (L51); PDF p. 112 "Barrie" (L53); PDF p. 118 "Informal Letters" (L56); PDF p. 122 "Conjunctions and Compound" (L58); PDF p. 126 "Question Tags" (L60).

# Sierra Leone — Language Arts Primary 5 Lesson Plans, Term 2 — FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03

## The book itself

- Title as printed on the cover: "Lesson plans for PRIMARY Language ARTS", with cover badges "5 CLASS" and "2 TERM", and "NOT FOR SALE" at the foot of the cover (PDF p. 1, cover image).
- Issuing authority as printed on the cover: "Ministry of Education, Science and Technology", with the coat of arms and "THE PRESIDENT'S RECOVERY PRIORITIES — Education" — the same cover, foreword (signed Dr. Minkailu Bah) and Introduction as Term 1, with only the class/term badges and contents changed.
- Year: no publication year is printed on the cover or in the foreword as read.
- PDF pages: 132. PDF p. 2 is blank. The numbering continues from Term 1 without a break: Lessons 61–120 (L-05-061 to L-05-120), every lesson 35 minutes, most lessons two printed pages (L67 and L68 share a tighter layout — L67 begins on printed p. 14 and L68 on printed p. 15).

## Lesson map — Term 2, Lessons 61–120, in order

- Lessons 61–72, Theme: Structure in Writing, then Gender and Verbs (from L71). A verb-heavy opening block. L61 Narrative Composition (subject–verb agreement inside a composition about the pupil's morning before school — "I wake up… My Mum gives me breakfast" type sentences); L62 Main Verbs (a past-tense text about Omar, who lived in Bo Town and preferred city life; pupils underline the main verbs); L63 Main Verbs and Helping Verbs; L64–L65 Irregular Verbs (past forms that do not add -ed; L65 works them through a story of a boy who finds money); L66–L67 Narrative Composition (planning, then writing, a composition about a good or bad personal experience); L68 Irregular Verbs again; L69 Main Verbs (a text about Suzan trying to be a better person by helping at home); L70 Compare Main Verbs (two boys who want to cook dinner); L71 Writing with Helping Verbs (do/did, am/is/are, was/were set — pupils write their own conversation); L72 Subject Verb Agreement.
- Lessons 73–75, Theme: Gender and Verbs. L73 Gender: Noun and Pronoun (why "Fatu… Fatu… Fatu" sounds wrong, and the pronoun that replaces a noun); L74 Identifying Different Genders in Language (in a reading text); L75 Writing with Different Genders (writing about people the pupils know).
- Lessons 76–80, Theme: Expressing Opinions About an Emerging Issue. L76 Present Continuous Tense (miming "You are swimming"); L77 Past Continuous (a family and what its members were doing); then the issue itself in three steps — L78 Expressing Opinions: Teenage Pregnancy (pair discussion of a good age to have a baby, and why), L79 Writing Opinions: Teenage Pregnancy, L80 Writing Opinions in Letters: Teenage Pregnancy (the opinion put into a letter to a friend).
- Lessons 81–85, Theme: Nonfiction Reading and Writing. A three-lesson study of one newspaper article on illiteracy: L81 Preparing to Read Factual Text (literate/illiterate/illiteracy; pupils frame questions first), L82 Reading for Answers (reading the article to answer their own questions), L83 Taking Notes (note-making from the same article); L84 Vocabulary: Using the Context (new words from an article about school in other countries); L85 Writing True Statements (writing about traditions in Sierra Leone; "tradition" is defined in the lesson).
- Lessons 86–90, Theme: Adjectives. L86 Review: Adjectives (an adjective describes a noun); L87 Interrogative Adjectives ("Whose pencil is this?"); L88 Possessive Adjectives ("your" pen); L89 Comparative Adjectives (describing Jesse against Abdul — taller/happier/more intelligent); L90 Superlative Adjectives (comparing more than two).
- Lessons 91–95, Theme: Essay Writing (L95 filed under the same theme in the contents). L91 Parts of an Essay (reading an essay about someone's favourite place); L92 Using Paragraphs in Essay Writing (writing two paragraphs about the pupil's own favourite place); L93 Planning an Essay (about a favourite person); L94 Essay Writing (writing that planned essay); L95 Poetry: Tone and Mood (a poem about the wind — tone and mood named and discussed).
- Lessons 96–100, Theme: Idioms; Writing. L96 Idioms (words that "mean something totally different" from what they look like, introduced through weather talk); L97 Writing with Idioms; L98 Narrating Details (telling family/friends about something interesting, surprising or shocking the pupil saw); L99–L100 Writing Semi-Formal Letters (planning, then writing in full, a semi-formal letter to a teacher about that event — a repeat-in-new-context of Term 1 L57).
- Lessons 101–105, Theme: Reading Comprehension; Verbs. L101 Comprehension and L102 Reading Comprehension (a two-part text about Mohamed and his problems with his older brothers and sisters); L103 Matching Verb Forms (was / will be across yesterday–today–tomorrow); L104 Writing With Verbs (diary writing — what a diary is for); L105 Verbs: Past Particles (title as printed in the contents, "Particles" [sic]; the lesson works on past forms of verbs for talking about the past — compare Primary 6 Term 1, where the same ground is headed "Participles").
- Lessons 106–110, Theme: Reading Comprehension. L106 Retelling a Story (a story carried by a set of past-tense verbs — lived, got, laughed, ran, shouted, left, picked, lit, rained, stopped — sequenced by the pupils); L107 Inferring About Main Characters (inferring what Amad — small, youngest, unhappy — is like, rather than being told); L108 Inferring About Main Characters (Continuation) (a second day of Mariama's diary — she was late because her sister overslept and they missed the bus); L109 Role Playing A Simple Plot and L110 Describe the Setting, both on "Little Red Riding Hood" (the wolf, the forest, the father saving her, in the book's own retelling).
- Lessons 111–115, Theme: Speculation; Expressing Opinions. L111 Differentiating Between Speculation and Information Questions (imagining, from a picture — would you like to own an airplane?); L112 Speculating (uniformed jobs — policeman, doctor and others — as the guessing material); L113–L115 Expressing Opinions: Protecting the Environment (reusing paper to save trees; what we must do; L115 turns the opinion into a letter to a friend, for a pupil whose friend has gone away and has no phone or internet).
- Lessons 116–120, Theme: Structure: Distributive Adjectives; Responses to Reading. L116 Word Meaning from Context (reading a letter from someone in Cairo, Egypt); L117 Lessons from a Passage (a story that teaches a lesson, discussed through friendship); L118 Responding to Text (pupils give their own thoughts on a story); L119 Distributive Adjectives (this/that first); L120 Distributive Adjectives in Compound Sentences (either, neither, both — joining the adjective work back to the compound sentence of Term 1).

## What a pupil is actually taught, and how

The grammar load rises sharply over Term 1: main vs helping verbs, irregular past forms, subject–verb agreement, noun/pronoun gender, present and past continuous, and five kinds of adjective (review, interrogative, possessive, comparative, superlative, distributive). Writing moves from composition to the essay as a named form with parts and paragraphs (L91–L94), and opinions are rehearsed on two live issues — teenage pregnancy and protecting the environment — in speech, in prose and in letters. Reading is mostly short board texts (Omar in Bo Town; Mohamed and his siblings; two diaries; Little Red Riding Hood) plus one real-genre text, the newspaper article on illiteracy, which is also the term's only note-taking lesson.

## Repeats and continuities

- Semi-formal letters (Term 1 L57) return at L99–L100 with a plan-then-write pair; comparative adjectives (Term 1 L21) return at L89 and are extended to superlatives at L90; compound sentences (Term 1 L58–L59) return inside L120; context-guessing (Term 1 L55) returns at L84 and L116; the story-retelling/inference sequence (Term 1 L16–L18) returns at L106–L108 in a comprehension key.

## Honest negatives — this book

- Still no phonics, handwriting, dictation or spelling rules; the one spelling-adjacent lesson is the mis-titled L105 "Past Particles".
- The "future" is never taught as a tense: "will be" appears only inside L103's verb-form matching.
- No formal letter is written this term (semi-formal only); no poetry is written (one poem is read, L95); no drama text or play script — role play is of a known folk tale.
- Teenage pregnancy is handled entirely as an opinion topic; the plans give no health or factual content about it beyond the discussion questions themselves.

## Citation check

15 concrete page anchors were checked against the actual PDF text and 15/15 passed, including: PDF p. 8 "Narrative Composition" (L61); PDF p. 10 "Omar lived in Bo Town" (L62); PDF p. 14 "Irregular Verbs" (L64); PDF p. 39 "teenage pregnancy" (L78); PDF p. 42 "illiteracy" (L81); PDF p. 50 "Review: Adjectives" (L86); PDF p. 60 "Parts of an Essay" (L91); PDF p. 68 "Poetry: Tone and Mood" (L95); PDF p. 80 "Mohamed" (L101); PDF p. 88 "Past Particles" (L105); PDF p. 93 "Amad" (L107); PDF p. 95 "Mariama" (L108); PDF p. 99 "Little Red Riding Hood" (L109/L110); PDF p. 111 "Cairo, Egypt" (L116); PDF p. 120 "Distributive Adjectives in" (L120).
